- **Source:** the thesis plan v3 `Plan_de_Tesis_UCSM_v3_Directiva.pdf` (119 pages, kept outside the repo), plus the decisions recorded below.

## Objective

Build a local, reproducible Python prototype that analyzes thematic redundancy and research gaps across the public defended theses of UCSM. Coverage is FCIFF, five programs, 2021–2026. The prototype includes a Streamlit interface for students and advisors.

It must also produce the evidence the thesis needs:
- a comparative experiment;
- labeled-set metrics;
- a temporal-split evaluation;
- expert validation.

## Problem / Why

Institutional integrity tools such as Turnitin detect textual overlap. They do not detect thematic redundancy, where two works tackle the same problem with the same approach but use different words. No tool maps the repository's research lines or surfaces unexplored areas (plan §1.1).

## Scope

**In:**
- Corpus acquisition from the public UCSM repository (DSpace 7.6.1, REST + OAI-PMH).
- Title, abstract, keywords, date, program and advisor taken from metadata; objectives parsed from the PDF.
- Two cleaning variants: light (for embeddings) and full (for TF-IDF / c-TF-IDF / coherence).
- Chunked multilingual SBERT embeddings and a TF-IDF lexical baseline.
- Topic map: UMAP + K-means vs HDBSCAN inside BERTopic, compared in a declared experiment.
- Direct-redundancy module with a calibrated threshold, a topic-saturation signal, and topic assignment for new proposals.
- Gap detection (four rules (a)–(d) plus a stability filter, with the OpenAlex "never touched" reference as a complementary external reference; D16 as amended by D32) and a recommender.
- Streamlit app with three views, and an Excel-based labeling workflow.
- Evaluation: internal clustering metrics, labeled pairs, temporal split, analysis of the expert questionnaire.
- A Dockerfile for the demo (M5–M6).

**Out (future work):**
- Production deployment, authentication, integration with UCSM systems (plan §3.1.2).
- A database server; persisting user queries.
- A full external literature corpus; fine-tuning models; BETO/MarIA.

## Constraints

- **Local only:** Windows 11, Python 3.13, CPU-only torch. No cloud processing.
- **Data stays out of git and images:** `data/` and `tessdata/` are gitignored and dockerignored.
- **License (CC BY-NC-ND 4.0):** research use only, no redistribution of texts. Every displayed thesis links to its repository item.
- **Privacy:**
  - Drop `renati.author.dni` and `renati.advisor.dni` at ingestion.
  - Never show author names in the app or reports; use codes.
  - Never persist user queries.
- **Language:** technical artifacts (code, comments, docs, tests) are in English. The UI copy language is pending (O01).
- **Remote operations need explicit user authorization per destination:** T04, T05 (repositorio.ucsm.edu.pe) and T25 (api.openalex.org).
- **Reproducibility:** `uv.lock`, fixed seeds, versioned configs, a frozen dated snapshot, and results tagged with config hash, git commit and snapshot id.

## Accepted decisions

| ID | Decision | Rationale / evidence |
|---|---|---|
| D01 | The corpus is public defended theses only; thesis plans are dropped | User decision 2026-10-02. No plans are public: 0 of 126 repository collections hold them |
| D02 | Census of all eligible theses (≈745), no sampling | 745 docs is computationally small, and sampling would create artificial gaps. Plan §3.3.3 must be updated |
| D03 | Metadata (title, abstract, keywords, date, advisor) comes from the API; the program comes from the collection; objectives are parsed from the PDF | Metadata keys verified 2026-10-02. There is no objectives field |
| D04 | Work from a frozen, dated local snapshot; update incrementally via OAI-PMH `from` | Reproducibility, availability, and politeness to the server |
| D05 | Storage is Parquet + `.npy` + PDFs on disk. No database in the MVP. Storage sits behind a port so a DB adapter can be added at deployment | Only ≈745 rows, the app is read-only, and labels arrive through Excel import |
| D06 | Stack as listed below; Python 3.13 with uv | PyPI wheels verified 2026-10-01. Python 3.14 is excluded because gensim 4.4.0 ships no cp314 Windows wheel |
| D07 | OCR uses PyMuPDF's built-in Tesseract plus tessdata files; no Tesseract install | Tested 2026-10-01 on a rasterized page using `spa.traineddata` only |
| D08 | Develop natively; add the Dockerfile at M5–M6 | Docker/WSL are not installed, and `uv.lock` already pins dependencies |
| D09 | Chunking: per section, sentence-aligned, ≤128 tokens under BOTH tokenizers. Section vector = mean of its chunks. Document vector = equal-weight mean of the sections, L2-normalized | Both models have `max_seq_length` 128. Vocab sizes differ (250037 vs 250002). Measured on 2026-10-11, those are the models' `config.json` `vocab_size` (embedding rows); the two tokenizers hold the same 250,002 tokens (see T13) |
| D10 | Strip the place/year suffix from titles. For TF-IDF, also remove domain stopwords and legal suffixes (S.A.C., E.I.R.L.) | 10 of the 20 most recent titles end with a place and/or year |
| D11 | Pass precomputed embeddings to BERTopic | Decouples BERTopic 0.17.4 (2025-12-03) from sentence-transformers 6 and transformers 5, both released later |
| D12 | Metrics: silhouette with cosine on the original embeddings; DB/CH on L2-normalized vectors; NPMI coherence on the top-10 c-TF-IDF terms; HDBSCAN noise excluded from internal metrics with the noise % reported; ARI for stability across seeds | sklearn's DB/CH accept only Euclidean distance |
| D13 | Report two separate signals: direct redundancy (calibrated τ) and topic saturation (informative only) | User decision 2026-10-02 |
| D14 | A new proposal is assigned to the nearest topic centroid (cosine, original space). No re-clustering at query time | Same rule for K-means and HDBSCAN. BERTopic's safetensors serialization drops UMAP/HDBSCAN |
| D15 | Labeled set (details below) | 20–30 pairs leave a recall margin of about ±0.28 |
| D16 | Gap rules: four rules (a)–(d) on the topic map plus a stability filter (present in ≥4 of 5 seeds), and the OpenAlex "never touched" reference (D17, T25) as a complementary external reference; experts are the final filter. Amended on 2026-10-20 by D32: it said "five types" without listing them | Plan §3.6.6 allows refining the criteria during development ("que se refinan durante el desarrollo") |
| D17 | "Never touched" reference: OpenAlex Topics (4516 topics, CC0), filtered by the subfields relevant to each program | The official research lines are too broad to reveal untouched topics |
| D18 | Official research lines are used as a coverage view | — |
| D19 | Code architecture is hexagonal-lite with a screaming layout. Notebooks only orchestrate | The experiment requires swappable components |
| D20 | Project repository: this repo, the single official repo for the whole system (remote `origin` on GitHub). No push without a user decision | Confirmed by the user 2026-10-02. One repo is enough; the user creates any extra repo on request |
| D21 | The earlier `TesisSimilitud` repo (Feb 2025) is ignored entirely | The user says it was only a test |
| D22 | Always construct BERTopic with `language="spanish"` (never the default) | With `embedding_model=None`, BERTopic 0.17.4 keeps `language="english"` and strips every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9 ]` before c-TF-IDF, so `fragmentación` becomes `fragmentacin`. Found and proven by the T02 smoke test |
| D23 | No backup copy of the raw snapshot; U7 is dropped | User decision 2026-10-03, risk R8 accepted. Partial mitigation: the metadata and PDF manifests record item uuids, bitstream uuids and sha256, so a later re-harvest can be diffed and documented |
| D24 | Deduplicate exact duplicate records before anything else | T07 found 2 pairs of `#tesis` records with identical title, abstract, author, advisor and date under different item uuids, so there are 744 distinct theses, not 746. T12 keeps the record accessioned first (`dc.date.accessioned`; a tie goes to the smaller item uuid) and logs the other as `duplicate_of`. Duplicates never enter the labeled pairs (T20) or the redundancy evaluation, where they would be trivial positives |
| D25 | OCR is selective: only low-text pages among the first `extraction.ocr_window_pages` pages (default 100) of a PDF are OCR'd. Other low-text pages keep their few text-layer characters, or are `empty`, with their low-text flag | The PDF text serves only the objectives (T10); title and abstract come from metadata (D03, D09). Measured 2026-10-03: the first text-layer page with "objetivo general" was found in 658 of 731 PDFs, at median page index 7, P95 25, P99 102. The window cuts OCR from about 8,600 low-text pages to about 1,960. It is configurable, so a wider pass can run later |
| D26 | Add ten research-action verbs to the domain stopwords: diseñar, desarrollar, implementar, elaborar, diagnosticar, seleccionar, validar, comparar, definir, medir, so the list goes from 79 to 89 lemmas. The outcome verbs mejorar, optimizar, reducir and incrementar stay out until the T16 review. The list is frozen before T18 | User decision 2026-10-09, from the T12 objectives re-check. Theses whose objectives hold each verb, of 728: diseñar 282, desarrollar 206, implementar 182, elaborar 124, diagnosticar 77, seleccionar 74, validar 67, comparar 65, definir 47, medir 39 (`results/fichas/20261002T224412Z/quality_report.json`). They play the role of the listed reporting verbs determinar (284), evaluar (282) and analizar (270). The nouns are separate lemmas (diseño 130, implementación 168), so their topic signal stays. Only the lexical side changes (TF-IDF, c-TF-IDF, NPMI), not the SBERT embeddings. T11a had removed `desarrollar` with a group that fit no criterion; it returns under the reporting-verb criterion. The outcome verbs may name a topic: mejorar 157, optimizar 141, reducir 86, incrementar 63 |
| D27 | T13 chunker design. C1: an in-house rule sentence splitter; `\n` is a hard boundary, and the abbreviation and enumerator protections each get a unit test. C2: a sentence too long for a chunk is split recursively at `;` or `:`, then at `,`, then into word-aligned hard windows, with no overlap and no truncation; every piece goes back to the packer. C3: the title is its own section and is never split; assert that it fits, and fall back to C2 if a future title does not. C4: a metadata-only thesis (16) takes the mean of its available sections, and `sections_used` is recorded per document. C5: both tokenizers are checked, through a sequence of token counters whose maximum counts, and the chunk manifest records each `tokenizer.json` SHA-256. C6: a cached vector is keyed by the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON `{model_id, model_revision, max_seq_length, normalize, text}`; the chunk table, keyed by (`doc_code`, `section`, `chunk_index`), goes under `data/interim`, never `results/`. C7: greedy packing in T13; balanced packing is left to T14's plain-mean sensitivity flag. C8: no `hypothesis` dependency: seeded random generators in plain pytest, a token-counter port with fake counters in unit tests, and a smoke test over the real corpus | User decision 2026-10-12, on the T13 design note measured on 2026-10-12 over the 744 included fichas of snapshot `20261002T224412Z`, offline. The note stays outside the repository, so its key numbers are below the table. Under C1 to C3 the snapshot gives 5,217 chunks (M), kept as a config expectation |
| D28 | T15 TF-IDF baseline design. C1: the input is the chunk sections (`section_texts` of `build_chunks`), each full-cleaned on its own by `clean_full_batch` with the 89 lemmas of D26; a section that cleans to no token is left out of the vectors and counted, a thesis with no term stops the build, and the doc codes, their order and each thesis's `sections_used` must equal the chunk table's, or the build stops. C2: two document vectors share one vocabulary and idf: `section_mean`, the primary one (each section's TF-IDF vector, L2-normalized, then the equal-weight mean of the sections, L2-normalized, as D09 does for SBERT), and `concatenated`, one vector over all the sections, the sensitivity variant. C3: unigrams and bigrams, the bigrams formed within each section only; `min_df` 2 theses (document frequency counted per thesis, not per section); `max_df` 1.0; sublinear tf; `l2` norm; smooth idf; no vocabulary cap; float64. C4: T15 persists the full-clean tokens per section, the terms with their document frequency and idf, and both matrices; it computes no c-TF-IDF, NPMI, clustering, reduction, pair pool or τ, and changes neither the cleaner's rules nor the stopword list. C5: the outputs go to `data/interim/<snapshot_id>/tfidf/`; the manifest, written last as the commit point, records the fichas and chunk table SHA-256, `tfidf_version`, `FULL_CLEANER_VERSION`, the spaCy model, both stopword hashes (spaCy's sorted list, and the sorted D26 lemmas with the list file's SHA-256) and each file's SHA-256; the verified reader checks each file before parsing it and refuses outputs whose fichas SHA-256, `tfidf_version`, `FULL_CLEANER_VERSION` or domain list differ from the current ones. C6: determinism through canonical CSR (`sort_indices()`) and uncompressed `save_npz`. C7: about 45 s per build, so the developer runs it. C8: a `tfidf` configuration block with an `expected` count gate. C9: test-first, with a fake cleaner port in unit tests, a privacy test, and smoke tests over the real corpus. C10: T20 draws the TF-IDF-high pool by rank, not by a cosine cut-off; the 0.95 sensitivity threshold is kept for parity with T14, and the manifest and the command's output also report the median and the minimum cosine between the two variants | User decision 2026-10-19: C1 to C10 accepted as written, plus the median and the minimum, since almost every thesis falls below 0.95 under TF-IDF. On the T15 design note, measured on 2026-10-19 over the 744 included fichas of snapshot `20261002T224412Z`, offline. The note stays outside the repository, so its key numbers are below the table |
| D29 | Under gap rule (c), plan §3.6.6 ("temáticas presentes en una carrera y ausentes en una carrera afín donde resultarían transferibles"), Ingeniería Electrónica can be the source of a transferable topic but never the target of a gap. Its theses stay in the corpus and in the redundancy search. `config/career_affinity.yaml` records it as `gap_target_excluded: [electronica]` | User decision 2026-10-19: Electrónica no longer admits students. Its included theses by year, from the fichas (M): 2021 16, 2022 8, 2023 3, 2024 5, 2025 4, 2026 10, 46 in all (D) |
| D30 | The T18 experiment declaration, fixed before any run, which makes O03 operational: the grid (E1), the choice of K and `min_cluster_size` on seed means (E2), the winner-selection rule, eligibility gates and then two stages (E3), the parameters that T17 left open (E4), the vectors of each metric (E5), the run records and the cost (E6), and the risks (E7). It is the `experiment` block of `config/default.yaml` with the values it reuses (the `umap`, `kmeans`, `hdbscan` and `seeds` blocks, `embedding.models`, and the `document_vector` of `embedding` and `tfidf`), pinned by `tests/unit/test_experiment_declaration.py` | User decision 2026-10-19: the T18 declaration proposal accepted as recommended, with its three user decisions UD1 to UD3 accepted as recommended. The proposal stays outside the repository, so its content is below the table |
| D31 | Agreement and validation statistics (T21, T30, T32), from the statistics notes N1, N2, N7 and N12. (a) The agreement checked against D15's target κ ≥ 0.6 is Fleiss' κ over all the labeled pairs, on the binary labels T22 uses (1 = redundant, after the mapping of the 3-level scale); Fleiss' κ on the 3 levels is reported as a complement, and Cohen's κ per pair of annotators, over the pairs they share, as a diagnostic only. (b) The 3 levels, their names and their mapping to the binary labels are declared in the labeling guide at T20, before the labeling round (proposal: only the top level counts as redundant); the T20 sampler spreads the pairs evenly over the pairs of annotators. (c) Success criterion (c) of the thesis plan §3.6.8 ("cada una de las cuatro dimensiones del cuestionario de validación obtiene una mediana igual o superior a cuatro") is read as the median over the experts of each expert's mean on the dimension's four items, counting only the experts who answered all four (`respondent_scores.median`); the median of the pooled answers and the item medians are reported as complements. (d) The "valor de aceptación" of §3.8.2 is met by an item when the lower bound of the 95 % score interval of its Aiken's V is ≥ 0.50 (`aiken_validity(..., criterion="ci_lower", cutoff=0.50, confidence=0.95)`), with V and both bounds reported per item | User decisions 2026-10-20. (a) Cohen's κ assumes the same two annotators over every item, while U3 rotates 3–4 advisors (≈80 pairs each, 2 per pair), so with 3 advisors each pair of annotators shares about 40 pairs (D); Fleiss' κ fits items rated by a fixed number of raters drawn from a pool (Fleiss, 1971), and the binary labels are T22's ground truth for τ and F1. Rejected: pooling "first slot vs second slot", and the same two annotators on all ≈120 pairs (Cohen's κ literally, but 50 % more work each, D). (c) The expert is the unit of analysis of a 6–8 panel, coherent with Cronbach's α per dimension; the pooled median counts one expert's four answers as four opinions, and the item reading is not what the criterion asks of a dimension. (d) Merino-Soto and Livia-Segovia (2009), *Anales de Psicología* 25(1), 169–171 (full text read), test the interval's lower bound against a standard: a liberal Vo = 0.50 (attributed to Cicchetti, 1994) or a conservative 0.70 or more (attributed to Charter, 2003), and advise the liberal one in the first phases of item construction with few judges (citing Penfield and Giacobbi, 2004). With 3 to 8 judges on a 1–4 or 1–5 scale, a lower bound ≥ 0.50 needs V of 0.688 to 0.889, and ≥ 0.70 needs V of 0.875 to 1.0, unanimity with 3 judges (M, `aiken_score_interval`, minimum passing V per panel) |
| D32 | Gap-rule declaration (T24), decided point by point before any gap rule ran on the real map. (1) The plan's rules (a)–(d) are primary; the thesis plan's literal (a) (topics under 5 % of the corpus) and (b) (mean distance above P75) are computed as a sensitivity table, never used for selection, together with `rare_max_share` 0.01 and `stability_min_overlap` 0.75. (2) D16 is amended: four rules plus the stability filter, with the OpenAlex reference (D17, T25) as a complementary external reference. (3) Rule (a)'s NPMI floor is the 0.25 quantile of the winner's per-topic NPMI over its 5 seed runs (D30 E4's primary setting), and (4) its size cut is the first quartile of the sizes of all those runs' topics, one cut for every run (`small_topic_cuts`); for C4 they are 0.1389 and 15.0, and if T22 changes the winner both are computed again by the same rule before any gap is looked at. (5) Rule (c): affine at a U4 score of at least 1, each gap carrying its pair's score so that the score-2 gaps form a high-confidence group; (6) present with at least 5 theses and 5 % of the source program's theses; (7) absent with 0 theses in the target. (8) Rule (d): a decline of at least 0.025. (9) Stability: a gap is present in another run at a Jaccard overlap of at least 0.5. Declared in `config/default.yaml` (`gaps`). 2026 covers the deposits up to the snapshot date, 2026-10-02, the study's cut-off | User decisions 2026-10-20, on a proposal grounded on the T19 C4 runs, reproduced exactly (labels, UMAP arrays and per-topic NPMI equal to the records). (1) The thesis plan assumed 250 to 300 documents (§3.1.2, PDF p. 68, printed 59), where 5 % is 12.5 to 15 theses; at 744 it is 37.2 and flags 10 of seed 42's 17 topics, and the literal P75 flags a fixed 25 % of the theses (186) by construction; §3.6.6 (PDF p. 78, printed 69) declares criteria "que se refinan durante el desarrollo". (2) No source lists five types; the PDF declares three criteria. (3) and (4) A cut per run moved from 14.0 to 17.0 across the seeds, so the stability filter mixed the topic's stability with the cut's: with one cut every gap found on seed 42 is present in all 5 runs (per-run cut: topics 2 and 5 stable; one cut: topic 2) (M). (5) U4 defines 0 as "not transferable"; with 2 only, Sistemas and Minas would leave rule (c). (6) At a 0.05 share, 0 theses in the smallest target (Sistemas, 65) has probability 0.036 under the source's share, and the one-sided Fisher test of the hardest pair, Electrónica (46) to Sistemas, gives p 0.027 for 4 theses and 0.011 for 5 (M); per cell and uncorrected, they set the scale only. (7) The thesis plan's "ausentes". (8) A one-sided 5 % two-proportion test at the median topic share, 0.0383, with windows of 360 and 384 theses detects a fall of 0.0232 (D). (9) Hennig (2007, *Computational Statistics and Data Analysis* 52, 258–271) treats a Jaccard of 0.5 or less as a dissolved cluster (the `clusterboot` documentation of his fpc package, read); seed 42 keeps the same stable gaps from 0.3 to 0.5 (M). With every value, seed 42 has 24 stable gaps: (a) 1, (b) 2, (c) 19 and (d) 2 (M) |
| D33 | Outputs of the real gap run (T24a). (1) The tracked records, under `results/gaps/<snapshot>/`, hold aggregates only: for each gap its rule, its run, its topics as `(run_id, topic)`, its programs, years, counts and shares, its affinity score, and the support and stability of the reference run's gaps, with no doc code. Which theses form each gap goes to `data/processed/<snapshot>/gaps/`, which git ignores, as T19's per-document labels do. (2) The tables name a topic by `(run_id, topic)` only and copy no term; a readable `summary.md` shows the top terms of the topics of each stable gap. Every tracked output passes the privacy guard in code (`sensitive_strings` and `leaked_strings` of `corpus/profile.py`: the snapshot's titles, abstracts and their long sentences, person names and ORCIDs) before it is written, and any hit refuses the write | User decisions 2026-10-21. (1) The README states that `results/` holds aggregates only, and T19 splits its outputs the same way. Rejected: the doc codes in the tracked records (each gap would point to individual theses in the repository pushed to GitHub), and every output ignored by git (the thesis's figures would have no committed evidence). (2) The terms are already committed in T19's `topics.parquet`, so the summary exposes nothing new, and the manual check of the terms that D30 declares before `topics.parquet` is committed becomes a check in code, which also holds if T22 changes the winner. Rejected: no term in any output (a gap cannot be read without joining T19's topics), and the terms with a manual check only |
| D34 | The labeled set's sampling and workbooks (T20), decided before any pair is drawn. (1) Name guard: T10b's name redactor stays as it is (T10c's policy: the letters-only boundary), and every text of a T20 workbook is also checked without word boundaries against the snapshot's person-name forms and ORCIDs (not its titles and abstracts, which the workbook shows); a hit stops the generation. (2) Blind labeling: each pair shows only the two fichas' title, abstract, objectives and keywords under a pair code, with no link to the repository page or the PDF and no author or advisor. (3) The scale: "Redundante" (mismo problema y mismo enfoque), "Relacionado" (mismo tema, distinto problema o enfoque) and "No relacionado"; T22's binary label is 1 for "Redundante" only (D31 (b)). (4) The universe: all the pairs of the 744 included theses, within and across programs, with no exclusion beyond D24; O09's pair stays and is flagged. (5) The pools, about 120 pairs: SBERT-high 40, 20 drawn from MiniLM's top 1 % and 20 from mpnet's; TF-IDF-high 20, drawn from TF-IDF's top 1 % by rank (D28 C10); mid-range 30, drawn from the pairs whose mean percentile rank over the three representations is at least 90 and below 99; random 30, uniform over the universe and unstratified. They are drawn in that order, and a pair drawn once is not drawn again. (6) Strata of the high and mid-range pools: the 5 programs and "between programs"; a pool's quota is proportional to its candidates per stratum, with at least 2 pairs for each stratum that has candidates, by largest remainder. (7) The pilot: 10 pairs, 2 or 3 per pool, drawn before the labeled set and excluded from it and from κ; they are labeled together in the calibration meeting, and their agreed labels are the guide's examples, at least one per level, in the main workbooks, which are generated after the meeting (this supersedes O02's 5 pairs). (8) The annotators: their number, 3 or 4 (U3), is a configuration value, confirmed by the user before the workbooks are generated; the pairs are spread evenly over the pairs of annotators (D31). (9) Disagreements: each disputed pair goes, in a short adjudication workbook, to an annotator who did not label it (with 3 annotators, the only one; with 4, rotated), and its final label is the majority of the three; κ is computed on the first two labels only | User decisions 2026-10-21, on read-only measurements over the 276,396 pairs of the 744 included theses (M). (1) The redactor's 3,096 name forms (every snapshot person; the shortest 9 characters, none a single word) match nothing in the 744 fichas' title, abstract, objectives or keywords, with or without word boundaries; over-redaction would change T10b's shared code for no gain here, and the guard stops a future glued name instead of passing it. The snapshot's `sensitive_strings` also holds every title and abstract, so the guard uses the name forms and ORCIDs only. (2) An advisor may know or have advised a thesis, and the repository page and the PDF show the authors. (3) The thesis's definition of redundancy (above, Problem). (4) The system compares a proposal against the whole corpus: 83,804 pairs share a program, yet 5 to 16 of each representation's 120 most similar pairs cross programs (106 for mpnet, 115 for MiniLM and 104 for TF-IDF share one); 3,994 pairs share an advisor, 10 of mpnet's top 120; the only pair that shares an author is O09's. (5) T22's labeled-set F1 decides between MiniLM and mpnet (UD3), so neither may pick the candidates alone, and each representation brings 20 high pairs; MiniLM's and mpnet's top 120 share 67 pairs, TF-IDF's shares 27 to 31 with either; the top 1 % (2,764 pairs) starts at 0.735 for MiniLM, 0.767 for mpnet and 0.151 for TF-IDF; drawing within it covers the region where τ falls, not only near-duplicates; 17,866 pairs have a mean percentile rank in the mid-range. Rejected: mpnet alone (circular), three high pools (D15 amended), the exact top 20 (τ calibrated where it is easy), 50/25/25/20 (fewer pairs where false positives arise). (6) Industrial holds 51 to 69 % of each top 1 %, Electrónica 3 to 34 pairs and Sistemas 10 to 63, so proportional quotas alone would leave them 0 or 1 pair in the SBERT pools; equal quotas would exhaust Electrónica. (7) R3 asks for 10; with 4 pools, 5 pairs leave a pool with one pair. Rejected: invented examples, and 3 real pairs labeled by the researcher. (8) U3 is still pending. (9) Rejected: a meeting of the two annotators, and the researcher deciding (the researcher's judgment would enter the ground truth of the researcher's own system) |

D15, labeled set, in detail:
- ≈120 pairs drawn from four pools: SBERT-high, TF-IDF-high, mid-range, and random.
- Sampling is stratified by program.
- Annotators use a 3-level scale; each pair gets 2 annotators.
- Target κ ≥ 0.6, checked as Fleiss' κ on the binary labels T22 uses; Cohen's κ per pair of annotators is a diagnostic (D31, 2026-10-20).
- τ is chosen by 5-fold cross-validation, with a bootstrap CI.
- SBERT is compared against TF-IDF.

D27, T13 chunker design, in detail. The numbers come from the design note, measured on 2026-10-12 over the 744 included fichas of snapshot `20261002T224412Z`, offline (`HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`), with its marks: M measured, D derived from measured values, E estimate.
- Sections (M), tokens with special tokens: titles, suffix stripped, median 32 and max 85, 0 of 744 above 128; abstracts median 366, 743 of 744 (99.9%) above 128; objectives median 157, 544 of 728 (74.7%) above 128. Both tokenizers give these exact figures.
- Tokenizers (M): identical input ids for every section text, 0 mismatches in 2,216 texts (744 titles, 744 abstracts, 728 objectives); the same 250,002 vocabulary entries and special-token ids; the two `tokenizer.json` files are byte-identical (9,081,518 B, SHA-256 `2c3387be…76b8`).
- C1, splitter (M), over 1,472 sections (744 abstracts and 728 objectives): the rule splitter took 0.04 s, the spaCy parser 11.68 s (+1.5 s load). The rule splitter leaves 0 chunks that end with an orphaned list marker (parser 89) and 0 boundaries without whitespace (parser 35). Against the parser, a reference and not a gold standard, its boundary precision is 0.997 and its recall 0.930. It gives 185 sentences over 126 content tokens, 168 + 17 (2.05%, D), against 161 for the parser.
- C2, over-long sentences (M): fallback (iv) gives 5,217 chunks, 103 more than truncation. Truncation would lose 8,977 tokens (2.14% of 419,478, D) in 143 documents and break the 128-token limit; hard windows with a 16-token overlap would add 212 chunks and repeat 3,910 tokens (D); fallback (i), without the comma level, adds 29 more chunks than (iv).
- C3, title (M): all 744 titles fit one chunk (max 85 tokens).
- C4, metadata-only theses (M): 16 (industrial 10, sistemas 3, mecanica 3), each with a title and an abstract and no objectives; under the mean of the available sections each section weighs 1/2 instead of 1/3. They give a median of 5 chunks per document (P95 7, max 10).
- C5, both tokenizers: about 4 s more per corpus run, roughly half of the 8 s cold run (E).
- C6, cache key: the revisions in the Hugging Face cache are MiniLM `e8f8c211…` and mpnet `4328cf26…`. Chunk table columns besides the key: `char_start`, `char_end`, `n_tokens` (the maximum over both tokenizers), `text_sha256` and `cut_kind` (`sentence`, `clause`, `comma` or `window`).
- C7, packing (M): greedy and balanced packing give the same 4,473 body chunks; balanced packing cuts the body chunks under 32 tokens from 146 to 20. Switching later costs about 2 s of chunking and one re-embedding of about 7 min (E).
- Expected count (M): 5,217 chunks, 744 titles and 4,473 body chunks, for snapshot `20261002T224412Z` under C1 to C3, with 0 chunks above 128 under either tokenizer recounted on its own. The note proposes keeping it as a config expectation, like `fichas.expected`, checked by a smoke test. T13a, which protects the period of `no` only before a number, measured the same count and a byte-identical chunk table.
- T14 cost (E): embedding 5,217 chunks takes about 102 s with MiniLM and about 328 s with mpnet, about 7.2 min for both.

D28, T15 TF-IDF baseline design, in detail. The numbers come from the T15 design note, measured on 2026-10-19 over the 744 included fichas of snapshot `20261002T224412Z`, offline, with its marks: M measured, D derived from measured values, E estimate.
- Inputs (M): 2,216 sections (744 titles, 744 abstracts, 728 objectives), 0 of them empty after full cleaning; 126,481 lemma tokens, a median of 164 per thesis (title 11, abstract 104, objectives 45); 9,466 lemma types, 4,961 of them in one thesis only.
- Vectorizer settings (M): under C3, 14,308 terms (4,505 unigrams and 9,803 bigrams), 98,282 nonzeros (density 0.92%), a median of 128 nonzeros per thesis (min 45), 0 zero rows. Unigrams only: 4,505 terms and 63,964 nonzeros; `min_df` 3: 7,249 and 84,164; `max_df` 0.5 would remove only the 6 frequent lemmas that T11 kept for the T16 review; joining the sections before forming bigrams would add 160 bigrams that span a section end.
- Variants (M): the cosine between a thesis's two vectors has a minimum of 0.776 and a median of 0.909; 719 of 744 theses are below 0.95 and 288 below 0.90; between the two pairwise-cosine matrices, Spearman 0.911 and a mean top-10 neighbour overlap of 0.672. T14 measured medians of 0.9765 (MiniLM) and 0.9822 (mpnet) for its two SBERT variants: the section weighting moves TF-IDF far more than SBERT, since under concatenation the abstract carries about 63% of a median thesis's tokens and the title 7% (D).
- Agreement with SBERT (M), pairwise Spearman: section mean 0.595 (MiniLM) and 0.582 (mpnet); concatenated 0.548 and 0.542. The concatenated variant shares 18 of its top 100 pairs with each SBERT model. TF-IDF pairwise cosines are an order of magnitude lower (P99 0.123 concatenated, 0.151 section mean; MiniLM 0.735, mpnet 0.767), hence the pool by rank (C10).
- Determinism and sizes (M): the same tokens, CSR arrays, vocabulary and idf across fits and processes; sklearn's output is not canonical until `sort_indices()`; uncompressed `.npz` files were byte-identical across fits and processes, 1,183,621 B per matrix.
- Cost (M): full cleaning of the 2,216 sections took 35.9 to 37.2 s over 3 runs; the vectorizer fit 0.26 s and the section-mean matrix 1.43 s. A build was estimated at about 45 s (E).
- The highest pair under both TF-IDF and mpnet is a near-duplicate that D24 kept; see O09.

D30, experiment declaration, in detail. The T18 declaration proposal was written on 2026-10-19 at HEAD `a6701e0`. Nothing was run on the real corpus: no clustering, topic model, UMAP or metric touched a real vector or text, and its costs come from synthetic data of the corpus's shape. Marks as in the proposal: M measured (in its probes on synthetic data, or recorded as measured in this plan), D derived from measured values, E estimate; a value with no mark is declared, a design choice with its reason.
- Thesis plan (`Plan_de_Tesis_UCSM_v3_Directiva.pdf`, PDF pp. 76–81, printed 67–72): UMAP is fixed a priori and PCA serves the figures only (§3.6.3); K-means and HDBSCAN run on the reduced embeddings inside BERTopic, whose c-TF-IDF gives each group's terms (§3.6.4); silhouette is the main internal metric, with DB and CH, and success criteria (a) to (d) follow (§3.6.8); two factors (MiniLM against mpnet, K-means against HDBSCAN) plus "TF-IDF con K-means", internal metrics on the original embeddings with the cosine, K by a silhouette sweep, HDBSCAN sizes "en valores representativos", UMAP at BERTopic's defaults, five seeded runs per configuration with mean and SD, and the winner by "el mejor desempeño conjunto" (§3.7).
- E1, the grid:
  - Representations: the `section_mean` document vectors of MiniLM and mpnet (D09, `embedding.document_vector`) and the TF-IDF `section_mean` matrix (D28, `tfidf.document_vector`), 744 rows each (M). The variants (`plain_mean`, `concatenated`) are sensitivity checks of the representation, not factors; a post-hoc sensitivity run of the winner alone stays possible and changes no selection.
  - Calls: one UMAP fit per (model, seed), cached and shared by K-means and HDBSCAN, so that their comparison is paired; BERTopic, built with `language="spanish"` (D22), only computes the c-TF-IDF from the labels, in its manual mode (`BaseDimensionalityReduction`, `BaseCluster`, `y` = labels). Evidence (M): BERTopic 0.17.4 passes `y` to UMAP and to HDBSCAN, and with `y=None` adds nothing; a seeded UMAP gave byte-identical output twice in one process and across two processes (synthetic 744 × 384); on synthetic data the manual mode kept noise as topic -1, kept the label partition one to one, and gave a c-TF-IDF matrix equal to `ClassTfidfTransformer` over directly counted classes. The same path serves the TF-IDF baseline, from precomputed labels (the T16 note).
  - UMAP: the `umap` block (`n_neighbors` 15, `n_components` 5, `min_dist` 0.0, cosine: BERTopic 0.17.4's defaults, M), with `random_state` = the run's seed and no sweep. With a `random_state`, umap-learn forces `n_jobs` to 1 (M), which makes it repeatable.
  - K-means: on the 5-D UMAP output, Euclidean; K from 8 to 50 in steps of 2, 22 values (D), with no extension after results (a maximum at a bound is reported as a finding). Reasons: below 8 the map has barely more groups than the 5 programs; at K = 50 the mean group holds 14.9 theses (D), the size of HDBSCAN's middle sizes; Grootendorst (2022, §5.3) evaluated 10 to 50 topics. `n_init` 10, explicit, instead of scikit-learn 1.9.1's `"auto"`, which runs one k-means++ initialization (M), so that the seed-to-seed spread reflects UMAP; it costs 9.71 s instead of 0.99 s per 110-run sweep (M, synthetic). The other arguments stay at the 1.9.1 defaults (k-means++, Lloyd, `max_iter` 300, `tol` 1e-4).
  - HDBSCAN: `min_cluster_size` {5, 10, 15, 20}; `min_samples` at its default, which is `min_cluster_size` in hdbscan 0.8.44 (M); BERTopic's defaults `metric="euclidean"` and `cluster_selection_method="eom"` (M). HDBSCAN is deterministic, so its seed-to-seed spread comes from UMAP alone.
  - Seeds: 7, 13, 21, 42, 73 (`seeds`). Each sets the `random_state` of UMAP and of K-means; the TF-IDF baseline uses it for K-means only.
  - TF-IDF baseline: K-means only, on the sparse L2-normalized `section_mean` matrix, with no UMAP (D28 C4, and "TF-IDF con K-means" in §3.7); its K by the same sweep and rule as E2, scored by the cosine silhouette on the sparse matrix, plus a matched-K run (E3).
  - Run count (D): 10 UMAP fits (2 models × 5 seeds); 370 clusterings, that is 220 SBERT K-means runs (2 × 22 K × 5 seeds), 40 HDBSCAN runs (2 × 4 sizes × 5 seeds) and 110 TF-IDF K-means runs (22 × 5), plus at most 5 matched-K runs; 25 final runs (5 configurations × 5 seeds), plus the at most 5 matched-K runs; c-TF-IDF and NPMI on at most 30 final runs, each at both NPMI settings.
- E2, the choices within a configuration, made on seed aggregates: one K (or size) per configuration, from the mean over the 5 seeds, so that ARI and the mean ± SD describe one partition size.
  - K\* is the K with the highest mean cosine silhouette over the seeds, on the original vectors (E5); an exact tie goes to the smaller K; the whole curve (mean and SD per K) is reported. The one-standard-error rule was not taken: §3.7 asks for a sweep scored by silhouette, read as the plain maximum.
  - HDBSCAN: among the sizes whose mean noise share is at most the noise cap of E3 (0.30) and whose metrics are defined in all 5 seeds, the highest mean silhouette wins; exact ties go to the lower mean noise share, then to the smaller size. If no size passes, HDBSCAN is ineligible for that model and is reported at its lowest-noise size, with its numbers.
  - Limitation, stated in the results: the choice and the report use the same runs, so the winning silhouette is slightly optimistic; with 744 theses and no held-out set, this is accepted.
- E3, the winner-selection rule: eligibility gates, then O03's order.
  - Configurations: C1 MiniLM + K-means, C2 MiniLM + HDBSCAN, C3 mpnet + K-means, C4 mpnet + HDBSCAN, and B0 TF-IDF + K-means, the baseline, each at its E2 value with 5 seed runs. Seed statistics are the mean and the sample SD (ddof 1).
  - "A beats B" on a seeded metric (silhouette, NPMI): `mean_A − mean_B > max(SD_A, SD_B)` in the better direction; otherwise the comparison is a tie. A difference within one seed SD is within run-to-run variation; with 5 seeds the standard error of a difference of two means with equal SDs is about 0.63 SD (D, √(2/5)), so the bar is about 1.6 standard errors.
  - Gates: G1, the three internal metrics are defined in all 5 seeds; G2, the mean noise share is at most 0.30, that is, at most 223 of the 744 theses without a topic (D, ⌊0.30 × 744⌋); G3, the mean pairwise ARI over the 10 seed pairs (D) is at least 0.65, with `own_label` noise handling. The gates screen for minimum adequacy: a pure lexicographic order would let a configuration that leaves 60% of the theses as noise win on silhouette, which D12 computes on the clustered points only.
  - Stage 1, the algorithm within each model's space (T19, no labels needed): (1) the gates: if only one configuration passes them all, it wins; if neither does, the one failing fewer gates goes on (a further tie continues to step 2), and the failure is a finding; (2) the silhouette (cosine, original vectors); (3) NPMI, at the primary setting; (4) the higher mean ARI (`own_label`); (5) the lower mean noise share; (6) on an exact tie, K-means, which gives every thesis a topic, as D14 assigns new proposals. The HDBSCAN silhouette with noise reassigned (E5) is recorded beside step 2; a flip under it is reported, and the rule is not changed.
  - Stage 2, the model (U3, then T21 and T22): (1) a model whose stage-1 configuration passes all the gates wins over one whose configuration does not; (2) labeled-set F1 from T22 (τ at the max-F1 point of the PR curve by 5-fold CV, D15), compared by a paired bootstrap over the labeled pairs, 2,000 resamples with seed 7, each model's F1 recomputed at its fixed CV-selected τ: if the 95% percentile interval of ΔF1 excludes 0, the higher F1 wins, otherwise it is a tie; (3) the NPMI of each model's stage-1 configuration, by the rule above; (4) MiniLM, the cheaper model: 127.2 s of encoding against 306.3 s for mpnet in T14's full run (M, progress log 2026-10-16), and 384 dimensions instead of 768. The winner is the stage-2 model with its stage-1 algorithm.
  - Provisional winner (UD3): T19 closes stage 1 and names a provisional winner by stage 2's steps 1, 3 and 4, marked provisional in every output; T22 closes stage 2 by the full rule. T23 to T26 may build on the provisional winner, and are rebuilt, in a few minutes (E), if the model changes.
  - The TF-IDF baseline is never a candidate; it enters acceptance criterion 1 only (UD1), read strictly. NPMI: the winner beats B0 by the rule above both at B0's own K\* and at a matched K, the winner's K\* when it is K-means, else the rounded mean number of HDBSCAN clusters over its 5 seeds, run for the 5 seeds (0 to 5 extra runs, D); NPMI depends on the number of topics, and §3.7 holds the other conditions constant. F1: the winner's F1 exceeds TF-IDF's, and the paired 95% bootstrap interval of the difference (the same resamples and seed) excludes 0. Partial outcomes are reported as findings.
  - Consequences: the app's map, T23 and the centroids use the winner's medoid run, the seed run with the highest mean ARI to the other four (a tie goes to the seed listed first in `seeds`); T24's stability filter (D16, at least 4 of 5 seeds) uses all five runs. Acceptance criterion 4 (§3.6.8 (d)) is met when the winner passes G3 and the coefficient of variation (SD / |mean|) over the 5 seeds is at most 0.10 for silhouette and for NPMI; it becomes unstable when a mean is near 0, which would itself signal weak structure. T27 reuses the winner's configuration and hyperparameters on 2021–2024 with no new sweep, so the temporal check (O04) tests assignment, not a new choice of K.
  - `selection.json` records every comparison with its values and the step that decided it.
- E4, the parameters that T17 left open:
  - NPMI unit: primary `document`, each thesis one document whose segments are its sections; sensitivity `window` with W = 10 (Röder et al.'s C_NPMI, and gensim's `c_npmi` default), reported and never used in the rule, with any flip of a stage-1 or stage-2 outcome reported. Reasons: the same unit as the clustering, with no parameter; theses are short, a median of 164 lemma tokens (M, D28), close to the windows of 50 tokens and more that Röder et al. (2015, §5) found to agree best with human ratings, so here a thesis behaves like a large window bounded by the thesis (D, by size, not tested). Windows of 10 give at least 106,537 units (D: 126,481 tokens − 9 × 2,216 sections); each section as a document (2,216 units) would lose the co-occurrence of a thesis's title and abstract. A call takes about 0.02 to 0.03 s (M, the T17 probe).
  - Reference corpus: all 744 included theses, the noise theses included, each one document whose segments are its full-clean sections (title, abstract, and objectives when present), read through `load_full_clean_sections` (T15) in the matrices' row order. This is intrinsic NPMI: it asks whether a topic's terms co-occur in this repository; an external Spanish engineering corpus is not available and is out of scope.
  - Top-N: 10 terms (D12; BERTopic 0.17.4's `top_n_words` default, M).
  - c-TF-IDF, comparable across the five configurations: the raw count of each T15 term (unigrams, and bigrams within one section) in each class, from T15's full-clean section tokens; the vocabulary fixed to T15's 14,308 terms for every configuration, B0 included, with no class-level `min_df` or `max_df`; the weighting of BERTopic 0.17.4's `ClassTfidfTransformer()` defaults (no BM25, no frequent-word reduction: each class row L1-normalized and multiplied by idf = log(A / f + 1), M); every label a class, -1 included, with topic -1 left out of NPMI and of the topic count; top terms read from the c-TF-IDF matrix, positive scores only, by score descending and then term ascending (code-point order). Each class has at least 45 positive terms (D), so 10 always exist. The route is T16's choice: BERTopic in manual mode with a fixed-vocabulary, section-aware `CountVectorizer`, or `ClassTfidfTransformer` on directly counted classes (equal matrices on synthetic data, M), with a parity test in T16. Reasons (M, BERTopic 0.17.4's code): a default `CountVectorizer(ngram_range=(1, 2))` would form bigrams across theses and sections and count its `min_df` over topics; `get_topic` pads with `""` and orders tied scores by an unstable sort. `y` is never passed together with a real UMAP model, since BERTopic forwards it to `umap_model.fit_transform` and so makes UMAP supervised.
  - Absent terms cannot occur under these settings (D: every vocabulary term occurs in at least 2 theses, and a bigram lies inside one segment), so an absent term is a pipeline mismatch and T19 stops with an error; T17's `None` and `absent_terms` path stays as the guard.
  - ARI noise handling: `own_label` for G3 and criterion 4, with `exclude_either` reported as sensitivity. `own_label` penalizes runs that disagree about which theses have a topic, and G2 bounds how far a large, consistent noise set can raise it; `exclude_either` ignores topic-or-noise flips, and two runs with different 30% noise sets would compare only about half the corpus. The two are identical for K-means. No literature standard was found.
- E5, the vectors of each metric, as D12, §3.7 (printed p. 71) and T17's code fix them: the cosine silhouette on the original vectors; DB and CH on the L2-normalized original vectors; HDBSCAN noise left out of the three, with its share reported; nothing computed on the UMAP space. The original vectors are the MiniLM and mpnet `section_mean` vectors (384 and 768 dimensions, M) and the TF-IDF `section_mean` matrix (744 × 14,308, M). The K sweep's silhouette is computed there too; no UMAP-space silhouette is reported, since §3.7 says it "infla artificialmente"; DB and CH are reported and not used in the rule; the sensitivity column `silhouette_noise_reassigned`, for HDBSCAN only, sends each noise thesis to the nearest cluster centroid by cosine (D14's rule; the centroid is the mean of the members) and scores all 744.
- E6, records and cost:
  - Under `results/experiments/<snapshot_id>/t19/`, which git tracks: `runs.parquet` (one row per clustering run, 370 to 375: `run_id`, `config_hash`, representation, model id and revision, vector variant, algorithm and its K or size, seed, clusters, noise points and share, the three internal metrics with `undefined_reason`, the reassigned-noise silhouette for HDBSCAN, the UMAP, clustering and metric seconds, and NPMI at both settings with the c-TF-IDF seconds for the final runs); `configurations.parquet` (seed means and SDs, the ARI mean, minimum and matrix under both noise handlings, the chosen flags); `topics.parquet` (final runs only: topic id, size, top-10 terms with their c-TF-IDF scores, topic NPMI and `absent_terms`; vocabulary lemmas, no thesis text); `selection.json` (every comparison, the deciding step, the winner, `provisional`, criteria 1 and 4); and `manifest.json`, written last (the git commit, refusing a dirty tree; the snapshot id; the SHA-256 of the embeddings, TF-IDF and fichas manifests; `declaration_sha256`; library versions; CPU; start and end times and total seconds). Under `data/processed/<snapshot_id>/experiments/t19/`: `labels.parquet` (`doc_code` × `run_id`, for T23, T24 and T27) and the 10 cached reductions, `umap/<model>__seed<seed>.npy`.
  - `config_hash` is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of a run's full parameter set, with `EMBEDDER_VERSION` and `TFIDF_VERSION`; `declaration_sha256` is the hash of this declaration, pinned by a unit test as D26 and U4 are. The commit that holds this decision, the configuration block and its pin test, made before T19 runs, is the timestamped pre-registration. T19 reruns one UMAP fit and one K-means run and compares their SHA-256 values (identical on synthetic data, M); before `topics.parquet` is committed, its terms are checked against the author names held in the fichas (zero expected).
  - Cost of T19, from synthetic data of the same shape on this machine (i7-12650H, 16 logical CPUs, M) or the T17 probe: imports about 50 to 70 s (E; M 22.5 s for umap, hdbscan, scikit-learn and T17, and 46.1 s for BERTopic, in separate processes); 10 UMAP fits 24.2 s (D: 11.26 s for the first, with JIT compilation, then 1.19 to 1.57 s each, M); 220 SBERT K-means runs 19.4 s (D, from 9.71 s per 110 runs, M); 40 HDBSCAN runs 0.6 s (D); 110 sparse TF-IDF K-means runs 58.68 s (M); 370 internal-metric calls 32.2 s (D: 0.038 s dense and 0.203 s sparse per call, M); at most 30 c-TF-IDF fits 40.5 s (D, 1.35 s each, M); NPMI and ARI about 2 s (D). In all about 4 min (E), or 4 to 12 min (E) allowing 2 to 3 times for real-data convergence: well under the 2-hour tool limit, so the developer runs it, and the command resumes per (model, seed). NPMI for every sweep run, which the rule does not need, would add about 8.3 min through BERTopic (D).
- E7, risks: X1 HDBSCAN's silhouette over clustered points favors discarding hard theses (G2, the reassigned-noise column, flips reported); X2 the silhouette maximum at K = 8 or 50 (a finding, no extension); X3 a late labeled set (the provisional winner); X4 most comparisons tie, so a fallback decides (declared ex ante; `selection.json` names the deciding step, reported in the results); X5 UMAP is repeatable on this machine, but not guaranteed across platforms or numba versions (library versions and CPU in the manifest; the repeatability check); X6 NPMI's agreement with human judgment may not hold for embedding-based topic models (Hoyle et al., 2021, as cited by Grootendorst, 2022, §5.3; the expert validation, T30 to T32, is the final check, and NPMI is one criterion among several); X7 selection and report on the same runs (a stated limitation); X8 BERTopic's pitfalls: cross-document bigrams, class-level `min_df`, `""` padding, unstable tie order, `y` reaching UMAP (E4's c-TF-IDF declaration and T16's parity test); X9 the PDF's §3.6.8 (a) asks for a silhouette comparison across spaces (UD1, then T34); X10 Industrial may dominate the topics (R5; the program composition per topic is reported, not a selection criterion); X11 B0 has no UMAP and the semantic configurations do (by design, §3.7 and D28 C4; B0 is compared only on space-independent metrics).
- User decisions, 2026-10-19, accepted as recommended:
  - UD1: acceptance criterion 1 governs: the semantic winner must beat the TF-IDF baseline on NPMI and on labeled-set F1; silhouette is reported within each space and never compared across spaces. The thesis plan PDF's §3.6.8 (a) (PDF p. 79, printed 70), which asks for a silhouette comparison against TF-IDF, contradicts its own §3.7 (PDF p. 80, printed 71) and must be corrected in the thesis document (T34 list; the related hypothesis wording in §1.4.1 too). The advisor should be told (U2 conversation).
  - UD2: the gates and constants are declared ex ante: mean ARI ≥ 0.65, mean noise share ≤ 0.30, coefficient of variation ≤ 0.10 for acceptance criterion 4. The 0.65 value is attributed to Steinley (2004) only through secondary sources (to be verified before citing); 0.30 and 0.10 have no literature source and are researcher-declared thresholds.
  - UD3: two-stage selection: T19 picks the algorithm per model and names a provisional winner; T22, with the labeled-set F1, confirms the model.
- Sources read by the proposal: this plan (Scope, Constraints, D09, D12, D14 to D16, D22, D24, D26 to D29, O03, O04, O09, U2, U3, the initial configuration, T16 to T24, T27, the T17 evidence and the acceptance criteria); `config/default.yaml`; T17's code; the installed sources of BERTopic 0.17.4, umap-learn 0.5.12, hdbscan 0.8.44 and scikit-learn 1.9.1 (versions M, from their `METADATA`); the thesis plan PDF, pp. 76–81; and the full texts of Röder et al. (2015, WSDM, §3.2, eq. 41, §5) and Grootendorst (2022, arXiv:2203.05794, §5.3). Open verification: the 0.65 / 0.80 / 0.90 ARI bands ("moderate / good / excellent recovery") are widely attributed to Steinley (2004), "Properties of the Hubert-Arabie adjusted Rand index", *Psychological Methods* 9(3), 386–396, whose bibliographic details were verified, but the bands were found only in secondary sources (the R package CrossClustering's `ari` documentation and a 2006 BMC Bioinformatics figure caption, by search on 2026-10-19). Its full text was not checked, so the attribution must be verified before it is cited; the bands were proposed for the recovery of a known partition and are used here by analogy. No literature value was found for the noise cap (0.30) or for the coefficient-of-variation cap (0.10); both are researcher-declared thresholds.
- Configuration (T18): the `experiment` block of `config/default.yaml` holds the values that no other block held (the K-means and HDBSCAN arguments, the baseline, the two choice rules, the c-TF-IDF, NPMI and ARI settings, the selection constants and the criterion-4 cap). It reuses, without repeating them, the `umap`, `kmeans` (the K sweep), `hdbscan` (the size grid) and `seeds` blocks, `embedding.models` (the fallback model must be one of them) and the `document_vector` of `embedding` and `tfidf`. `AppConfig.experiment_declaration_sha256()` hashes all of these as canonical JSON; T19 writes that hash as `declaration_sha256`, and `tests/unit/test_experiment_declaration.py` pins it.

## Open decisions

| ID | Question | Proposal | Status | Needed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| O01 | Language of the UI copy | Spanish, because the end users are UCSM students and advisors. Code and docs stay in English | **Accepted 2026-10-02** | T28 (M5) |
| O02 | How annotators label pairs | Run a short calibration meeting (guide + 5 pilot pairs together). Then each annotator fills one Excel workbook at home: guide sheet, side-by-side texts, dropdowns, randomized order, no system scores shown. We import and validate it. No server or DB needed | **Accepted 2026-10-02** | T20 (M3) |
| O03 | How the winning configuration is chosen | The embedding model is chosen by labeled-set F1, then NPMI. The clustering algorithm is chosen by within-space silhouette, then NPMI, stability and noise coverage. The rule is declared before T19 | **Accepted 2026-10-02**; made operational by D30 (2026-10-19) | T18 (M3) |
| O04 | How to measure success on the temporal split | Assignment consistency between the 2021–2024 map and the full map, plus a sample rated by experts | **Accepted 2026-10-02** | T27 |
| O05 | Acceptance criterion for objectives extraction | ≥90% correct on a manual, stratified sample of ~60 theses. Otherwise fall back to title + abstract and document it | **Accepted 2026-10-02** | T10 |
| O06 | Rule for flagging an atypical proposal | Flag it when its similarity to the nearest centroid is below the P5 of member-to-own-centroid similarities | **Accepted 2026-10-02** | T23 |
| O07 | Where the expert demos run | The local laptop, with the Docker image ready. Revisit at M5 | **Accepted 2026-10-02** | T29 |
| O08 | PR chain strategy | Work units go directly on `main`, with no PR chain. The user makes every commit and push manually in GitHub Desktop. The developer prepares each verified work unit and announces when a commit is due, with the file list and a Conventional Commit message | **Accepted 2026-10-02 (user decision; revised the same day)** | First commit |
| O09 | Two included theses, `DOC-1b29cde4` and `DOC-b2303c69` (program mecanica, 2021), look like one thesis deposited twice: identical objectives, advisor, author codes, program, year and keywords; a different title, abstract and PDF, so D24's exact-match rule kept both. They are the most similar pair under TF-IDF (cosine 0.9958) and under mpnet (0.9905), per the T15 design note | Options were (a) widen D24 and rebuild the fichas and every output after them, or (b) keep both theses. The user opened both PDFs and confirmed that they are the same thesis, deposited twice by the repository after an error in the first deposit. The parent checked that this is the only such case among the 744 included theses (M, 2026-10-19): it is the only group of included theses with identical objectives (after whitespace and case normalization) and the only group with the same author set; no two included theses share a title or an abstract; it is the only pair above 0.98 cosine under either model (`section_mean`: mpnet 0.9905, MiniLM 0.9898), and the next pairs are at 0.947 under mpnet and 0.9503 under MiniLM. **Decision: keep both.** D24 stays unchanged, and the pair is not excluded from T20 or T22; it is a known true duplicate (a real re-deposit in the repository), so T22 flags it and its results mention it | **Decided 2026-10-19 (user decision)** | T20 |

The same conversation tells the advisor about D30's UD1: acceptance criterion 1 replaces the silhouette comparison against TF-IDF of the PDF's §3.6.8 (a) (T34). **The population change was approved by the advisor, reported by the user on 2026-10-19.** Whether the advisor was also told about D30's UD1 was not reported, so that part stays pending | Before T19 (M3) |
| U3 | 3–4 advisors as annotators (≈80 pairs and 2–3 h each) | T21 (M3) |

U4, career-affinity matrix, in detail. It feeds gap rule (c) of plan §3.6.6 and was fixed on 2026-10-19, before any topic result existed (T16 has not run). The file is `config/career_affinity.yaml`, and `tests/unit/test_career_affinity.py` pins its pairs by a SHA-256 of their sorted `a-b=value` lines, as the D26 test pins the stopword list. Marks: M measured, D derived from measured values.
- Scale: 0 no affinity (topics of one program are not transferable to the other), 1 partial, 2 high. The matrix is symmetric over the five program keys; `mecanica` is one repository collection covering Mecánica, Mecánica-Eléctrica and Mecatrónica. Under D29, `electronica` is never the target of a rule (c) gap.
- Procedure: the researcher (the user) proposed a score per pair from shared-course evidence; three advisors, anonymized as Asesor 1, Asesor 2 and Asesor 3, reviewed each pair (agree, or their score and reason, or "cannot judge"); the final score is the majority among the advisors who judged.
- Provenance: the advisors' answers were provided by the user. They were collected in one shared workbook, so the three answers were not independent. The workbook and its supporting evidence are held by the user outside the repository.
- Agreement (D, derived by the parent from the workbook): 19 of 28 advisor judgments agreed with the proposal (67.9%), plus 2 "cannot judge". The majority raised three pairs from the proposed 0 to 1, and in each the dissenting advisors agreed on 1: sistemas–electronica (2 of the 2 who judged), sistemas–mecanica (2 of 3) and industrial–minas (2 of the 2 who judged). Minority corrections that did not change the score: sistemas–industrial (one advisor proposed 2), sistemas–minas and electronica–minas (one advisor each proposed 1).
- Evidence for the proposal (M, measured by the parent on 2026-10-19 from public curricula, course names only). Sources: the 2026 curricula published on the UCSM pages of Sistemas, Industrial, Mecánica, Mecánica Eléctrica, Mecatrónica and Minas, and Electrónica's study plan 74202104 ("vigente desde 2021", PDF). Distinct courses per curriculum: sistemas 63, industrial 68, mecanica 62, mecanica electrica 62, mecatronica 63, minas 63, electronica 67; the mechanical group's union has 100. The 11 courses common to every 2026 curriculum (general training) were not counted. Inside the mechanical group, shared courses: Mecánica–Mecánica Eléctrica 34, Mecánica–Mecatrónica 28, Mecánica Eléctrica–Mecatrónica 28.
- Caveat: identical-name counts are a lower bound, since schools name related courses differently; that is why the advisors' judgment decides. The counting scripts and the downloaded pages stay outside the repository.

| Pair | Final score | Shared courses by identical name, case and accents ignored: specialty + basic science (M) |
|---|---|---|
| sistemas–industrial | 1 | 1 + 3 |
| sistemas–electronica | 1 | 0 + 3 |
| sistemas–mecanica | 1 | 0 + 4 |
| sistemas–minas | 0 | 0 + 2 |

## Architecture and data layout

```
MIGUEL-VALDIVIA-SIMILITUD-TESIS/
├── pyproject.toml · uv.lock · .python-version
├── config/                      # YAML: paths, ocr, chunking, models, umap, grids, seeds, thresholds, gaps
├── data/                        # gitignored
│   ├── raw/<snapshot_id>/       # metadata.jsonl, pdfs/, manifest.json (immutable)
│   ├── interim/<snapshot_id>/   # fichas, exclusions, chunks (T13), embeddings/ (T14), tfidf/ (T15)
│   ├── processed/<snapshot_id>/ # maps/<config>/, gaps/
│   ├── cache/chunk_vectors/     # raw chunk vectors per model (T14)
│   └── labels/                  # annotator workbooks (out/in), labels.parquet
├── results/                     # experiment tables and figures (no raw texts)
├── tessdata/                    # spa/eng traineddata (gitignored, fetched by script)
├── src/thematic_redundancy/
│   ├── corpus/                  # harvesting, bitstream selection, snapshot manifest, ficha schema
│   ├── extraction/              # PDF text, OCR fallback, objectives locator
│   ├── preprocessing/           # light/full cleaners, stopwords, title suffix stripping
│   ├── representation/          # chunker, embedder port + adapters, tfidf
│   ├── topic_map/               # umap, clustering adapters, bertopic wrapper, assignment
│   ├── redundancy/              # similarity search, threshold calibration, saturation
│   ├── gaps/                    # gap rules, stability filter, openalex reference
│   ├── recommendation/          # antecedents (MMR), alternative lines
│   ├── evaluation/              # clustering metrics, coherence, PR/F1, kappa, temporal split, likert
│   ├── labeling/                # pair sampler, excel workbook io
│   └── shared/                  # domain types, config models, storage ports + parquet adapters
├── experiments/                 # notebooks/scripts that only call src/
├── app/                         # Streamlit (map, query, gaps)
├── tests/                       # unit, fixtures, smoke
├── docker/                      # Dockerfile (M5–M6)
└── odd/tasks/                   # this document
```

The chunk table (T13), the embeddings (T14) and the TF-IDF matrices (T15) live under `data/interim/<snapshot_id>/`, beside the fichas they derive from, not under `processed/`, so one interim directory serves every reader and staleness check (D27 C6, D28 C5); `processed/` keeps the topic maps and the gaps.

## Stack

Versions were verified on PyPI on 2026-10-01 and 2026-10-02. Major versions get pinned after the T02 smoke test.

Python 3.13 · uv · pymupdf 1.28 · pdfplumber 0.11 (fallback) · httpx 0.28 · pandas 3.0 · numpy 2.5 · pyarrow 25 · pydantic 2.13 · pyyaml · spacy 3.8 + es_core_news_md 3.8.0 (URL dependency) · sentence-transformers 6.1 · transformers 5 · torch 2.14 (CPU) · umap-learn 0.5.12 · hdbscan 0.8.44 · scikit-learn 1.9 · bertopic 0.17.4 · gensim 4.4 · scipy 1.18 · pingouin 0.7 · statsmodels 0.15 · openpyxl 3.1 · streamlit 1.64 · plotly 7.1 · matplotlib 3.11 · seaborn 0.13 · jupyterlab 4.6 · pytest 9.1 · ruff

## Initial configuration (declared ex ante; every change is logged here)

| Key | Initial value |
|---|---|
| snapshot filter | `dc.date.issued` from 2021 to 2026, `renati.type` = tesis, the 5 program collections |
| programs.collection_uuid | sistemas `695b14ab-e5b2-49b5-9edf-f709e883b73b`, industrial `f0217548-ce48-4bae-b163-21b5e2504ef6`, electronica `a8bdd7c0-f1aa-4797-b04e-112e27f60da3`, mecanica `cf0ca97e-9b81-484d-a325-611b8a8d8223`, minas `9d9ecbe5-56c5-4cdf-81a3-b9a3736a47b3` (added in T04) |
| repository | `page_size` 100 (1–1000), `request_interval_seconds` 1.0 (1.0–60.0), `max_retries` 3 (0–10) (added in T04) |
| ocr | `spa+eng`, 300 dpi, `full=True` on pages without a text layer |
| extraction | `min_text_chars` 50 (≥1): a page under 50 stripped characters is low-text. `ocr_window_pages` 100 (≥0; 0 turns OCR off) (D25). (added in T09) |
| extraction.header_footer | `edge_lines` 3 (1–10), `min_share` 0.3 (>0–1), `min_pages` 5 (≥2): a line among the first or last 3 non-empty lines of a page that repeats on at least 30% of the pages with text, in a PDF with at least 5 such pages, is a running line (added in T09) |
| objectives | `general_max_chars` 1200 (≥1), `specific_max_chars` 4000 (≥1), `min_chars` 40 (≥1, below `general_max_chars`): longer texts are cut and flagged, a shorter general objective is flagged (added in T10) |
| objectives.verification_sample | `per_program` 12 (≥1), `seed` 20261007 (0 to 2^32−1), `workbook_dir` `data/labels/objectives_check` (relative, no `..`, and inside `paths.data_dir`, since the workbook holds thesis text) (added in T10) |
| fichas | `wrong_pdfs`: `20.500.12920/11777` carries the PDF of `20.500.12920/11776`. `expected`, for snapshot `20261002T224412Z`: 766 records, 744 included, 2 duplicates, 20 non-thesis, 728 with objectives, 16 metadata-only; the counts must add up, and the build stops when one is missed (added in T12) |
| chunk.max_tokens | 128 under both tokenizers, special tokens included |
| chunking.expected | for snapshot `20261002T224412Z`: 744 documents, 5,217 chunks, 744 title chunks and 4,473 body chunks; the counts must add up, and the chunk build stops when one is missed (added in T13, D27) |
| doc_vector | equal-weight mean of the section vectors, L2-normalized |
| models | paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2, paraphrase-multilingual-mpnet-base-v2 |
| tfidf | `max_ngram` 2 (unigrams, and bigrams within each section), `min_df` 2 theses, `max_df` 1.0, `sublinear_tf` true, `document_vector` `section_mean` (or `concatenated`), `sensitivity_cosine_threshold` 0.95. `expected`, for snapshot `20261002T224412Z`: 744 documents, 2,216 sections, 14,308 terms, 98,282 nonzeros; the build stops when one is missed (added in T15, D28) |
| umap | `n_neighbors` 15, `n_components` 5, `min_dist` 0.0, metric cosine. A separate 2D map for visualization. In the experiment, `random_state` is the run's seed, and one fit per (model, seed) is shared by K-means and HDBSCAN (D30, T18) |
| kmeans.k | sweep 8–50, step 2 (22 values), scored by the mean cosine silhouette over the seeds on the original vectors; an exact tie goes to the smaller K. Range confirmed in T18 (D30), with no extension after results |
| hdbscan.min_cluster_size | {5, 10, 15, 20}, unique and ascending, with `min_samples` left at its default (D30, T18) |
| seeds | 5 fixed seeds: 7, 13, 21, 42, 73. Each sets the `random_state` of UMAP and K-means (D30, T18) |
| experiment | the D30 declaration (added in T18): K-means `n_init` 10 and `init` k-means++; HDBSCAN `min_samples` default, metric euclidean, `cluster_selection_method` eom; the TF-IDF baseline as K-means on the sparse matrix with a matched-K run; K and `min_cluster_size` chosen on seed means; c-TF-IDF over T15's vocabulary; NPMI over the top 10 terms by thesis as document (windows of 10 as sensitivity), an absent term stops the run; ARI `own_label` (`exclude_either` as sensitivity); gates mean ARI ≥ 0.65 and mean noise share ≤ 0.30; ties within the larger seed SD; F1 by a paired bootstrap of 2,000 resamples with seed 7 and a 95% interval; fallbacks K-means and MiniLM; the medoid run as the representative; criterion 4's coefficient of variation ≤ 0.10. Pinned by `tests/unit/test_experiment_declaration.py` with the blocks it reuses |
| redundancy.tau | max-F1 point of the PR curve, via 5-fold CV |
| saturation | quartiles of topic size |
| recommender | top-k 5, MMR λ 0.7, 3 neighbor topics, 3 nearby gaps |
| gaps.a | bottom quartile of topic size, plus an NPMI floor, each cut once over every seed run of the winner: the first quartile of their topic sizes and the 0.25 quantile of their per-topic NPMI (D32; C4: 15.0 and 0.1389) |
| gaps.b | pairs among each topic's 3 nearest topics with ≤1 bridge document |
| gaps.c | per `config/career_affinity.yaml` (U4, fixed 2026-10-19; D29); affine at a score of at least 1; present with at least 5 theses and 5 % of the source program; absent with 0 theses in the target (D32) |
| gaps.d | change in share between 2021–2023 and 2024–2026, minimum count 3; a decline of at least 0.025 (D32) |
| gaps.stability | present in ≥4 of 5 seeds, at a Jaccard overlap of at least 0.5 (D32) |

  - use strict types (no string-to-number coercion).

  - Behavior notes:
    - `k_values()` returns the inclusive sweep.

      - A new status, `unverifiable`, in `PdfStatus` (`corpus/pdf_manifest.py`): the PDF cannot be checked against the repository's listing, and asking again would not change that. It is not in `FAILURE_STATUSES`, so it does not count toward the stop after 3 failures in a row (it breaks such a run, like any standing outcome), and it is not retried. (Changed by the review fixes below: such outcomes in a row now have a stop of their own, any of them sets exit code 1, and `--recheck-unverifiable` fetches them again.)
      - A checksum in an algorithm that cannot be verified is now `unverifiable` (`unsupported_checksum_algorithm`), still without any download; it was `integrity_error`.
      - A PDF whose size or checksum differs from the listing is downloaded once more. If the second download sends the very same bytes (the same size and SHA-256 of the bytes kept), the thesis is `unverifiable`, with reason `size_mismatch` or `checksum_mismatch` and a detail that says the listing looks stale. Different bytes give `integrity_error`, with the second download's reason; a second download that passes is kept as `downloaded`. (Narrowed by review fix F1: a body longer than listed is always `integrity_error`.)
      - A body that is not a PDF stays `integrity_error`, with no second download: an error page served in place of the file is its usual cause, and it may pass.
      - The checking sink kept and hashed at most the listed size, so that a body longer than listed was described the same way whatever chunks it arrived in. (Removed by review fix F1, which no longer compares such bodies.)
      - A later run lists an `unverifiable` thesis again (one request) and downloads it only once the listing changes: another bitstream, size, algorithm or checksum. (Review fix F2 adds `--recheck-unverifiable` to fetch one sooner.)

(Review fix F1 removed the code of two of them: the overflowing chunk and the header of a longer body.)
    - Review fixes (2026-10-12): an independent adversarial review raised a blocker (F1), a warning (F2), and two smaller findings for this task (F5, F6). One scoped correction fixed them, test first, with F2's design decided by the parent overnight. **The user confirmed that design on 2026-10-13**, by committing `7c6a4e1` after it was explained.
      - F1, blocker, a false stale listing. For a body longer than listed, the two downloads were compared on their first listed bytes only, so the right file followed by a trailer that changed was recorded `unverifiable`, with a false "listing looks stale" detail, and never retried; the README claimed otherwise. Now a body longer than listed is always `integrity_error`: no second download, retried by the next run, and counted toward the stop after 3 failures. Only a body shorter than listed, or a same-size checksum mismatch, that a second download repeats byte for byte may become `unverifiable`. The detail texts and the README say so. RED with the review's probe scenario (the right file plus `<trailer A>`, then plus `<trailer BBBB>`; and the same trailer twice): 3 failed, 71 passed (`unverifiable` where `integrity_error` was expected). GREEN: 74 passed. The probe, rerun: `integrity_error`, 1 download. The probe's test replaces the one that pinned the removed sink code for longer bodies.
      - F2, warning, a safety regression. A server or proxy that sends the same wrong file for every bitstream used to stop the run after 3 `integrity_error` with exit code 1; with `unverifiable`, every thesis was downloaded twice, the run never stopped, exited 0, and nothing was fetched again later (review probe: 16 downloads, exit 0). The parent's overnight decision, now implemented:
        - (a) `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_MISMATCHES` = 3 theses in a row that the run's own downloads found `unverifiable` stop the run with their own message: the server or a proxy in front of it may be sending wrong files; check what it sends before running again. An `unverifiable` outcome without a download in this run (an unknown checksum algorithm, or an entry kept from an earlier run) neither counts nor breaks that run of theses, so a rerun is not blocked by theses that an earlier run already recorded; any other outcome breaks it. `ItemOutcome.downloads` (0, 1 or 2) carries what the count needs.
        - (b) Any `unverifiable` thesis in a run makes the command exit with 1 (the module has no distinct code for problems), with a last line such as `2 theses are unverifiable (checksum_mismatch 1, unsupported_checksum_algorithm 1): … --recheck-unverifiable`.
        - (c) `--recheck-unverifiable` (`recheck_unverifiable=True`) downloads the `unverifiable` theses again even when their listing is unchanged, still twice at most each.
        - RED (with the new constant added first, so that the RED shows behavior): 8 failed, 73 passed: 3 `AttributeError` for `downloads`, 3 exit codes 0 where 1 was expected, 2 `DID NOT RAISE DownloadStoppedError`. GREEN: 81 passed. The review's probe through the command line, rerun: the first run stops after 3 theses and 6 downloads, exit 1; a rerun against a fixed server downloads the other 5 and exits 1 (3 still `unverifiable`); `--recheck-unverifiable` downloads those 3 and exits 0.
      - F5: the test named `…_neither_stop_the_run_nor_break_a_run_of_failures` is now `test_an_unverifiable_thesis_breaks_a_run_of_consecutive_errors`, and asserts only that (for an unknown algorithm and for a stale listing). It passed at once; a mutant that leaves the failure count as it is after an `unverifiable` outcome fails it.
      - F6: the README claimed that a rerun keeps a matching PDF on disk "whatever the manifest held for it before", which does not hold for a thesis refused with HTTP 401, 403 or 404 or closed by its `dc.rights`: `fetch` returns those entries first. The README now says so.
      - Mutants, each with a fresh bytecode cache and the module byte-identical after each restore, all killed (11): a longer body described for comparison; mismatches in a row never stopping; outcomes without a download counted; outcomes without a download resetting the count; other outcomes keeping the count; `--recheck-unverifiable` ignored by the fetcher; downloads not counted; exit 0 with `unverifiable` theses; the last line without its reasons; the option not passed on; an `unverifiable` outcome neutral in a run of failures (F5).

      - exclusion iff not included;
