- [x] **T08a** Ficha/storage follow-ups from the T08 reviews (non-blocking):
  - Key creation must not depend on hard-link support. `os.link` fails on FAT/exFAT, some SMB shares and some container bind mounts, which matters for T29.
  - fsync the key's parent directory after publishing it.
  - The storage schema check should cover nullability and list item types, with tests for wrong list item types and for non-list columns.
  - Route D. Risk H, raised from M for this unit, since it handles the pseudonym key and the storage contract. Trigger: two source modules and their two test modules (4 non-trivial files), plus this plan, the ledger and the README.
  - Evidence (2026-10-12):
    - Item 1, key creation without hard links (`corpus/anonymize.py`, `load_or_create_key`):
      - The hard link stays where it works: the key is staged, flushed and linked into place, so it appears complete or not at all.
      - Any other link failure than an existing file leads to the fallback: `O_CREAT | O_EXCL` with mode `0o600`, then the write and an fsync. A FAT volume gives EPERM on Linux and EINVAL (winerror 1) on Windows; some shares give EOPNOTSUPP or winerror 50. Since the codes vary by system, the fallback takes any `OSError`; exclusive creation still never overwrites a file. A failure before the whole key is in the file removes the partial file; a later one keeps it (review fix W1 below).
      - A key file that is short, or (review fix W3) holds 32 zero bytes, may still be in writing by another run, so it is read again for up to `KEY_SETTLE_SECONDS` (2 s) and then refused, never replaced. A longer file is refused at once. `clock` and `sleeper` arguments, as in `corpus/dspace.py`, replace real time in tests.
      - A run that loses the race uses the winner's key. If the winner fails and removes its partial file, the loser raises `FileNotFoundError` and the next run creates the key.
      - Residual risk, in the docstring: a crash during the in-place write can leave a short file or, on FAT or exFAT, a full-length file of stale or zero bytes. Short and all-zero files are refused; other stale bytes cannot be told apart from a key.
    - Item 2: the run that publishes the key then fsyncs its directory with a local `_fsync_directory`. A directory that cannot be opened (Windows) or flushed (EINVAL, or any `OSError`) is skipped, since the key is already flushed and named. A run that loses the race, or reads an existing key, flushes nothing. `experiments/domain_stopwords.py` keeps its own helper, which T11b fixes.
    - Item 3 (`shared/storage.py`): `_check_columns` compares each column's nullability with its field's, both ways, and `_same_type` compares the type and the nullability of list items, whatever their name (Parquet reads `item` back as `element`). Exact nullability was chosen because the store writes it from the model, so a difference means another model version or another writer.
    - RED → GREEN:
      - Storage: 3 failed, 26 passed (`DID NOT RAISE` for list items that may be null, a nullable column for a required field, and a required column for an optional field) → 29 passed. A wrong item type and a plain column for a list were already refused; those 2 tests are characterization.
      - Key, counted over the whole module `test_anonymize.py` (the tests kept from HEAD included): 24 failed, 69 passed → 93 passed. Of the 24: 9 `TypeError` for the new `clock` argument (the settle tests, and the wrong-size test now on a fake clock), 9 with the link error propagating, 2 with that error instead of the simulated disk-full one, and 4 missing the directory steps. Three new tests passed at once, as characterization: an interrupted link leaves nothing, and reading an existing key or losing the race flushes nothing.
      - Written after GREEN: a test where the winner gives up (above), and a race of 4 real threads over 20 rounds, with and without hard links. A probe saw more than one creator in 200 of 200 rounds in both modes, but never a short read: 0 settle sleeps in 200 rounds of each mode. So the settle loop is covered by the fake-clock tests, not by the race test.

They covered no fallback, a fallback on EPERM only or on interruptions, no `O_EXCL`, mode `0o666`, no removal or removal on `Exception` only, a loser that keeps its own key (exclusive, link, vanished file), no settle wait, a wait for long files or past the settle time, no directory fsync, an fsync before the publish, after a lost race or on an existing key, directory open or fsync errors raised, the descriptor not closed, the staged file kept, the fallback key not fsynced; and in storage, item nullability, column nullability (either way), the item type or the list check ignored, and swapped messages.

    - Review fixes (2026-10-12): two independent adversarial reviews of this unit, one scoped correction.
      - W1 (warning, shown by both reviews with probes). The fallback removed the key file after any failure, even once all 32 bytes were visible. A run that read the key in that moment kept using it, while the next run created another key. Now the file is removed only while it holds fewer than 32 bytes on disk; after that, it is kept and the error is raised. Reading the size on disk rather than setting a flag also covers a stop between the flush and the flag, and a key that the closing of the file flushes.
      - W2. On Windows the removal fails while another run holds the file open, and its `PermissionError` replaced the original error. Errors of the removal are now suppressed; the short file stays, and the next run refuses it.
      - W3. The docstring now says that a crash during the in-place write on FAT or exFAT can leave a full-length file of stale or zero bytes. A file of 32 zero bytes counts as incomplete during the settle wait and is then refused with a `ValueError`. The key format is unchanged, so the real key stays valid.
      - W4. The docstring now says that mode `0o600` is requested: it binds on POSIX file systems, while FAT, exFAT and CIFS mounts take their mount options. A POSIX-only test checks `0o600` for a key made either way; it is skipped on this Windows machine, so it has not run here.
      - W5. A test pins that a short key file that disappears during the wait gives way to a new key.

Passed at once, as characterization: the vanishing short file, and the 4 partial-write cases (0 or 16 bytes visible) that must still remove the file.

- [x] **T09b** Follow-ups from the T09 and T09a reviews (non-blocking). Item 1 is done, and the user decided item 2 on 2026-10-12: option (a), so the task is closed.
  - Item 1 (done): move the OS lock and the atomic JSON write, which `extraction/` repeats from `download_pdfs` and `pdf_manifest`, into `shared/` and reuse them (from T09).
  - Item 2 (decided: option (a)): edge lines of digits only (from the T09a review):
    - A running footer made of digits only, such as `2024` on every page, is kept on every page.
    - A year with punctuation, such as `2024.` or `– 2024 –`, is only partly protected, because the line key folds the digits. It goes only when its folded key (`#.` or `– # –`) reaches the running-line threshold, counting the year: that key must sit at the edges of at least `max(2, ⌈min_share × pages with text⌉)` pages, in a document of at least `min_pages` pages with text (declared: `min_share` 0.3, `min_pages` 5). The pages counted are the year's own page and every page with an edge line that folds to the same key, such as `– 12 –` (a page number) or `12.` (not one). So the year goes when it repeats on enough pages, or when enough numbered lines share its key. It stays when they are on too few pages, as in a test with a share of 0.5: of 10 pages, the year with `– 2 –` and `– 3 –` puts the key on 3 pages of the 5 needed. It also stays when no other line shares its key.
    - It is harmless for the objectives: T10 met O05 with 60/60.
    - Why it was the user's decision: any change to this behavior changes the page records of PDFs already extracted, so it must raise `EXTRACTION_VERSION`, which extracts all 731 PDFs again. The T09 full run took 1 h 29 min; at the slower OCR speed seen in T09a, its OCR alone would take about 6.5 h. T10's locator and T12's build would then run again (seconds each), and their tables would need to keep their SHA-256, or their changes reviewed. That cost is the user's decision.
    - Options:
      - (a) Keep the current rules, now pinned by tests (below). Nothing runs again.
      - (b) Compare an edge line that is a 4-digit year, bare or with punctuation, by its exact text instead of its folded key: a year that repeats on enough pages goes, a one-off year stays, and page numbers still go as page numbers. Other numbered lines keep today's rule; compared by exact text, running numbering such as `12.` on every page would no longer repeat, and would stay. Raise `EXTRACTION_VERSION` to 3 and run the extraction again.
      - (c) Extend the digits-only guard to a 4-digit year with punctuation, so that it is kept like a bare year. A running year footer then stays on every page, as `2024` does now. The guard must stay limited to years: widened to any digits with punctuation, it would also keep running numbering such as `12.` on every page, which goes today only as the running key `#.`. Same re-extraction.
    - **Decision (user, 2026-10-12): option (a).** The current digits-only edge-line rules stay, pinned by the 9 characterization tests below. `EXTRACTION_VERSION` stays 2, and no PDF is extracted again, so T10's locator and T12's build need no new run.
  - Route D. Risk M. Trigger: a new shared module and its test module, 8 source modules and 5 test modules moved onto it or pinned, plus this plan and the ledger.
  - Evidence (2026-10-12):
    - Item 1, `shared/filesystem.py`:
      - `exclusive_run(directory, busy_error)` is a context manager that holds the OS lock on `<directory>/.lock` (`LOCK_FILE_NAME`) without waiting: `msvcrt.LK_NBLCK` on Windows, `flock(LOCK_EX | LOCK_NB)` elsewhere. When another run holds it, it raises the caller's own error from the system's `OSError`, so `download_pdfs` and `extract_text` keep `DownloadInProgressError` and `ExtractionInProgressError` with their messages. The lock file stays; the lock is released when the block ends, also on an exception or Ctrl+C, and by the system when the process dies.
      - `write_text_atomically(path, text)` replaces `text_manifest.write_json_atomically` and the copy inside `write_pdf_manifest`. It writes the text plus one line feed, in UTF-8 and in binary mode (no CRLF), to `<name>.part` beside the target; it flushes and fsyncs it, then calls `os.replace`. On any failure or interruption it raises the original error and removes the staged file where the system allows it (review fix F2). Same bytes as before.
      - Added: after the replace, `fsync_directory` flushes the directory, tolerating failures exactly as `_fsync_directory` in `corpus/anonymize.py` does. A directory that cannot be opened (Windows) or flushed (EINVAL, or any `OSError`) is skipped, and the descriptor is always closed. A rename is durable on POSIX only once its directory is flushed, as `experiments/domain_stopwords.py` and the key already do. On this Windows machine the step is skipped, so the files written are unchanged. Side effect: in `build_fichas`, the manifest's directory flush also covers the two tables renamed into that directory just before.
      - Callers moved: `corpus/download_pdfs.py` and `extraction/extract_text.py` (the lock, and the extraction summary), `corpus/pdf_manifest.py`, `extraction/text_manifest.py`, `extraction/locate_objectives.py`, `labeling/objectives_check.py`, `corpus/fichas_quality.py` and `corpus/build_fichas.py`. The two lock copies, the two writer copies, and the imports of the writer from `extraction/` into `corpus/` and `labeling/` are gone. No re-export was needed: nothing outside these modules used them. `test_build_fichas.py` now patches `build_fichas.write_text_atomically`.
      - Left as they are, as possible later reuse, since they write other kinds of files: the workbook save in `objectives_check.py`, the Parquet replace in `shared/storage.py`, `extraction/ocr_assets.py`, and the PDF download's partial-file replace. `_fsync_directory` in `corpus/anonymize.py` now repeats `fsync_directory`. The one in `experiments/domain_stopwords.py` does not quite: it lets an `os.fsync` error propagate after the output is published, which T11b fixes. Reusing `fsync_directory` there changes that experiment's failure behavior, as noted under T11b.

The lock is checked from a second handle and from a child process: refused at once with the caller's error and its `OSError` cause, released after the block, after an error and after Ctrl+C, not blocked by a lock file left behind, and freed when a holder process is killed. The writer is checked for its exact bytes, the replace, a staged file left behind, a missing directory, the order fsync → replace → directory flush, the directory steps that fail, and failures or interruptions of the fsync or the replace.

Lock (7): another file name, no `OSError` cause, a waiting lock (`LK_LOCK`, caught by the 2 s bound of the child-process test), a generic error, a held lock ignored, and a lock file kept open after an exception. Writer (10): no final line feed, text mode, cp1252, no fsync, staged file kept on failure or staged elsewhere, no directory flush, a failed directory flush or a directory that cannot be opened raised, the descriptor not closed. Callers (6): a changed download message, the snapshot or the parent directory locked, either manifest without indentation, the Markdown report's trailing line breaks kept. The survivor is equivalent on CPython: dropping both the `with` and the `finally` still releases the lock on an exception, because the generator's frame is cleared and the file closed. - [x] **T10b** Fixes from the slice-2 reviews of T10: two warnings and one suggestion, all non-blocking.
  - Route D. Risk M.
  - Trigger for route D: a module fix with tests, plus the plan, the ledger and the README.
  - Warning 1, an unreadable workbook escaped the import command as a raw traceback. `main` caught only `WorkbookValidationError`, `OSError`, `ValueError` and `yaml.YAMLError`, and openpyxl raises other exceptions for a damaged or non-xlsx file.
    - Fix: `import_verification` loads the workbook through `_load_workbook`, which turns those exceptions into one `ValueError`: "`<path>` is not a readable xlsx workbook (`<reason>`)". The command prints it as one `error:` line and exits with code 1. A missing file is still a `FileNotFoundError`. The exceptions, as probed on openpyxl 3.1.5:
      - `BadZipFile`: garbage bytes or a truncated zip;
      - `KeyError`: a zip without the workbook parts;
      - `SyntaxError`: broken XML, since `ParseError` is a subclass;
      - `TypeError`: a value of the wrong type in a part;
      - `InvalidFileException`: an extension openpyxl does not support.
    - RED: 5 command-line cases (garbage, truncated zip, zip without a workbook, broken XML, invalid value) escaped as `BadZipFile` twice, `KeyError`, `ParseError` and `TypeError`. GREEN: 5 passed.
    - `InvalidFileException` comes only from openpyxl's extension check. The command never reaches it, since the workbook name is fixed, so a direct test imports an xlsx saved under a `.xls` name. It was added after GREEN and is proven by its mutant below.
    - 5 throwaway mutants, one per exception class dropped from the list, were all killed and then reverted.
  - Suggestion, two refusal branches of `import_verification` had no test: a row whose handle is not in the sample, and a row whose `Estado` was edited.
    - Added one characterization test per branch. Each asserts its exact problem message.
    - Both passed at once, as characterization tests do. 4 throwaway mutants were all killed and then reverted: each problem not recorded, the unknown-handle message without its row, and the status check inverted.
  - Warning 2, the name redactor matched without word boundaries, ignoring case, so a name form could match inside a longer word of the objective text.
    - Fix: a form is replaced only where no letter stands right before or after it. The boundary is a lookaround on `_LETTER`: a word character other than a digit, `_` or a superscript digit, so accented letters and ñ count as letters. Python's `\w` is Unicode-aware for `str` patterns.
    - Why letters, not `\w` or `\b`:
      - A digit glued to a name, such as a footnote number (`1` or `²`), must not stop the redaction. With `\w` lookarounds it did, which would leak the name.
      - A form that ends with an initial's period needs no letter after it, but `\b` would demand one. One name of the snapshot ends that way.
      - With `re.ASCII`, `ñ` would end a word.
    - RED 1, the old pattern: a synthetic family name that ends one longer word and begins another came out as `pre[nombre omitido] con la [nombre omitido]ña`.
    - RED 2, after a first fix with `\w` lookarounds: two names followed by a footnote number were left unredacted.
    - GREEN: all passed.
    - 6 throwaway mutants were all killed and then reverted: the lookbehind dropped, the lookahead dropped, ASCII-only `\b`, Unicode `\b`, `\w` lookarounds, and superscript digits counted as letters.
    - No effect on the completed verification. The workbook holds 0 redaction markers, so the old pattern matched no form anywhere in its 60 rows. Every match of the new pattern is also a match of the old one, so a rebuild would redact 0 cells too. The workbook was not rebuilt.
  - Every mutant ran with a fresh bytecode cache (`PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX`), and the module was byte-identical after each restore.
  - Checks: `pytest` 1,107 passed, 1 skipped (1,097 + 10 new: CLI 5, extension 1, characterization 2, redactor 2); `ruff check` and `ruff format --check` clean.
  - Commit: `c121d78`, made by the user together with the T10 closure. Approved before and after the commit, so the reviewed boundary is now `c121d78`.

### P3 — Representation and topic map (M3–M5)
- [x] **T13** Sentence-aligned chunker that is valid for both tokenizers. Property tests check that every chunk fits both tokenizers and no text is lost.
  - Route D. Risk M. Route declaration (2026-10-15): delegated direct, one writer. Trigger: 2+ non-trivial files: four new modules under `representation/`, three new unit test modules and a smoke module, the configuration model and its tests, plus this plan and the ledger.
  - Inputs (2026-10-11): both models are cached (U8), so the chunker can run offline (`HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`). Measured offline with transformers 5.18.0: the MiniLM tokenizer (model type `bert`) and the mpnet tokenizer (`xlm-roberta`) hold the same 250,002-token vocabulary, with the same 5 special tokens, and gave the same token ids for 3 Spanish sample sentences with accents, ñ and symbols. D09's 250,037 vs 250,002 are the models' `config.json` `vocab_size`. Keep the check under both tokenizers, since it is cheap and guards a model swap; comparing both tokenizations over the whole corpus would confirm the equality.
  - Input (2026-10-12, T07a): over the metadata of snapshot `20261002T224412Z`, the two tokenizers give the same token count, special tokens included, for each of the 7,097 distinct texts that the metadata profile tokenizes: titles, abstracts, abstract sentences and the empty text. Counts were compared, not token ids. The PDF objectives were not compared.
  - Input (2026-10-12, D27): the accepted design, C1 to C8, with its measured numbers under the decisions table. The design note compared the token ids of all 2,216 section texts, objectives included: 0 mismatches (M). Expected count for snapshot `20261002T224412Z`: 5,217 chunks (744 titles and 4,473 body chunks, M), kept as a config expectation like `fichas.expected`.
  - Tests proposed by the design note. Unit tests, with fake counters and seeded generated texts: every chunk fits every counter, two disagreeing counters included; no text lost, with ordered, non-overlapping spans; chunks aligned to sentences, with cuts inside an over-long sentence in the order `;`/`:`, `,`, window (read from `cut_kind`); pathological input (a no-space run of more than 300 characters, a word longer than the limit, a whitespace-only section, a `None` section); greedy maximality; determinism; stability on its own output; a title of at most 128 tokens is one chunk; splitter cases (`S.A.C.`, `Dr.`, `etc.`, decimals, `1.` and `a)` kept with their item, `\n`, `¿` and `¡`, quotes, and the documented limits of a lone initial and of a period before a lower-case word). Smoke tests, real models offline: 0 chunks above 128 under either tokenizer and the text recovered for all 2,216 sections, through `load_fichas`; the expected count; tokenizer equality, or matching recorded `tokenizer.json` SHA-256 values.
  - Evidence (2026-10-15). Every value was measured in this unit unless marked D (derived).
    - Modules, hexagonal: a pure core, a port, an adapter, and the IO at the edge.
      - `representation/sentences.py`, C1: `sentence_spans`, the rule splitter, with `ABBREVIATIONS` (73 entries: the design note's list without `et al`, which never matched, since a word holds no space). Every line break is a boundary.
      - `representation/chunker.py`, the pure core: the `TokenCounter` port and `MaxTokenCounter`, which keeps the largest count (C5); the units, with the C2 cuts (`;` or `:`, then `,`, then the longest word-aligned windows that fit, found by bisection and cut inside a word only when that word alone does not fit); greedy packing (C7); `chunk_title`, one unit that falls back to C2 (C3). Nothing overlaps or is truncated, and each chunk records its `cut_kind`.
      - `representation/hf_tokenizers.py`, the adapter: it loads the tokenizer from the folder of the cached `tokenizer.json` with `local_files_only=True`, and records the model id, the cache revision, the file's SHA-256 and the vocabulary size. A cache without `tokenizer.json` is refused, so a partial cache cannot count with a stand-in tokenizer (U4-03 under T07a).
      - `representation/build_chunks.py`, the IO edge: the section texts (title: `clean_light`, then `strip_title_suffix`; abstract and objectives: `clean_light`), the chunk rows, the checks, the table and its manifest, the verified reader `load_chunks`, and the command `uv run python -m thematic_redundancy.representation.build_chunks`, wired like `build_fichas`.
    - Outputs, under the gitignored `data/interim/<snapshot>/`:
      - `chunks.parquet`, one row per chunk, keyed by (`doc_code`, `section`, `chunk_index`), with `char_start` and `char_end` (the span in the section text), `n_tokens` (the largest count), `cut_kind`, `sections_used` (C4, the same in every row of a thesis), `text_sha256` and `text`. The `text` column is an addition to D27's list, so that T14 reads each chunk without rebuilding the section texts.
      - `chunks_manifest.json`, written last as the commit point: the snapshot, the time, `chunker_version` 1, the fichas table's SHA-256, `max_tokens`, each tokenizer (model id, revision, `tokenizer.json` SHA-256, vocabulary size, and the largest chunk that it counts on its own), the table's SHA-256, the counts, the chunks per `cut_kind`, the theses per `sections_used`, the sections recovered, and the seconds. No text.
    - Checks inside the build, which writes nothing when one fails: each chunk counted again by each tokenizer on its own, at most `max_tokens`; every section recovered from its chunks, with only whitespace around and between them; the counts equal to `chunking.expected`.
    - Configuration: `chunking.expected` (`ChunkCountsConfig`), for snapshot `20261002T224412Z`: 744 documents, 5,217 chunks, 744 title chunks and 4,473 body chunks. `chunks` must equal `title_chunks + body_chunks`, and `title_chunks` must be at least `documents`. The snapshot-id pattern is now shared with `fichas.expected`.   - Evidence (2026-10-16). Every value was measured in this unit unless marked D (derived) or E (estimate).
    - Modules, hexagonal as in T13: a pure core, a port, an adapter, and the IO at the edge.
      - `representation/vectors.py`, the pure core: the `SentenceEncoder` port, with `EncoderIdentity` and `EncoderSettings`; `pool_chunk_vectors`, which gives the section means, the `section_mean` document vector (the equal-weight mean of the sections in `sections_used`, D09 and C4) and the `plain_mean` one (the mean of all the chunks, C7), both L2-normalized; and `variant_agreement`, the cosine between the two.
      - `representation/vector_cache.py`: the C6 key (`canonical_key_json` and `cache_key`), `model_slug`, and `ChunkVectorCache`, one file per model.
      - `representation/st_encoder.py`, the adapter: it loads the model from the folder of its cached `modules.json` with `local_files_only=True`, takes the revision from the snapshot folder (T13's `snapshot_revision`), pins the torch threads, and returns the raw float32 output (`normalize_embeddings=False`). A lookup that returns `None` or raises `OSError` is a missing model (compare T12c item 11), and a model outside the cache layout is refused, since the key needs its revision.
      - `representation/build_embeddings.py`, the IO edge: `embed_texts`, `check_counts`, `build_embeddings`, the manifest, the verified reader `load_embeddings`, and the command `uv run python -m thematic_redundancy.representation.build_embeddings`, wired like `build_chunks` (exit 0, 1, or 130 after an interruption).
    - Outputs, under the gitignored `data/interim/<snapshot>/embeddings/`:
      - `embedded_sections.parquet`, one row per section, the row order of every section matrix: `doc_code`, `document_row`, `section`, `n_chunks`.
      - Per model, in `<model id with "/" as "--">/`: `documents_section_mean.npy` and `documents_plain_mean.npy` (one L2-normalized row per thesis) and `sections.npy` (one raw row per section), float32.
      - `embeddings_manifest.json`, written last as the commit point: `embedder_version` 1; `chunker_version` and the chunk table's SHA-256; the cache-key scheme and directory; the encoder settings; the configured `document_variant`; the sections table's SHA-256; the counts (documents, sections, chunks); and per model its id, revision, `max_seq_length`, dimension, `normalize` false, output SHA-256 values, cache file, distinct texts, texts from the cache, texts embedded, embedding seconds, and the cosine summary of the two variants. No text.
      - `load_embeddings(interim_dir, model_id, variant=None)` checks every file against the manifest before parsing it, and gives the manifest's `document_variant` unless asked for the other.
    - Checks, which stop the build before any output is written: the documents and chunks must equal `embedding.expected` before any model loads; each model must have `max_seq_length` 128 (D09) and read the longest chunk whole; each encoder must return one finite vector of its dimension per text.
    - Configuration: `embedding` gains `batch_size` 64, `device` `cpu`, `threads` 10, `document_vector` `section_mean` (or `plain_mean`), `sensitivity_cosine_threshold` 0.95, and `expected` (`EmbeddingCountsConfig`: snapshot `20261002T224412Z`, 744 documents, 5,217 chunks; `chunks` must be at least `documents`).
    - Implementation notes, for the user to confirm (not D rows):
      - Raw chunk vectors are averaged, and only the document vector is L2-normalized: D09 normalizes the document vector, so the section means keep the model's own scale, and the cache serves both variants.
      - Cache: `data/cache/chunk_vectors/<model id with "/" as "--">.npy`, gitignored and outside any snapshot, so a later snapshot reuses unchanged chunks. One NumPy structured array (`key`, 64 hex digits in ASCII; `vector`, float32) sorted by key, so its bytes are deterministic; no text; written atomically after every 1,024 new vectors, so an interruption loses at most one checkpoint; a damaged file is refused, never rebuilt silently.
      - Sensitivity threshold 0.95: a round cut-off declared before the full run, which only counts the theses whose two document vectors differ noticeably; it changes no vector.
      - Batch size 64 and 10 threads: the design note's cost estimate was measured with batches of 64 on 10 torch threads, the default on this 10-core CPU; pinning the threads keeps them the same across runs.
    - Test first:
      - RED, against stub modules (empty or zero results; `NotImplementedError` for the adapter, the build, the reader and the command) and the new configuration fields without their check: `uv run pytest tests/unit/representation tests/unit/test_config.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` gave 73 failed, 470 passed, 1 skipped; `tests/unit/representation` alone 72 failed, 288 passed. The failures: 24 assertion errors, 16 `DID NOT RAISE`, 7 `FileNotFoundError` (the stub cache wrote no file) and 26 `NotImplementedError`.
      - GREEN: 543 passed, 1 skipped, after one test's wrong expectation was fixed: a thesis with one chunk per section has the same vector under both variants, so 2 of its 3 theses, not 3, fall below that test's threshold.
      - Refactor under green: the count gate became `check_counts` and the per-model step `_embed_model`; the interruption message now says that the cache keeps the vectors saved at each checkpoint. The first build test gained a check of the keys and the raw vectors in the cache, since no test pinned the key that the build uses.
      - The new tests: 20 pooling cases (`test_chunk_pooling.py`), 28 cache cases (`test_vector_cache.py`), 4 adapter cases with stand-in modules (`test_st_encoder.py`), 22 build, reader and command cases (`test_build_embeddings.py`), 14 configuration cases, and 4 smoke tests (`tests/smoke/test_embeddings.py`).
    - Mutants: 18 throwaway mutants, each restored byte-identical (the SHA-256 of the 4 new modules checked after the run), with no bytecode written: all killed. Pooling (4): the section mean over chunks; the plain mean over sections; no L2 normalization; "below" as `<=`. Cache (4): unsorted key JSON; escaped key JSON; missing keys not deduplicated; repeated keys accepted in a file. Build (8): `normalize` true in the key; no checkpoint save; `from_cache` counted per chunk; no count gate; no 128 check; no truncation check; the reader's default variant fixed; the reader skipping a matrix's SHA-256. Adapter (2): normalized output; threads ignored.

  1. `representation/build_embeddings.py:553-574` (R3-001, warning, in both reviews): `load_embeddings` checks every output file against `embeddings_manifest.json`, but never checks that manifest against the current chunk table. No test covered this drift.

    - Item 1. `load_embeddings` calls a new private helper, `_check_current(manifest, interim_dir)`, right after it reads `embeddings_manifest.json` and before it reads any table or vector. The helper reads the current chunk manifest through `chunks_manifest` and compares four fields: `snapshot_id`, `chunker_version` and `chunks_sha256` with the chunk manifest's, and `embedder_version` with the code's `EMBEDDER_VERSION`. It compares the two manifests and does not hash `chunks.parquet` again, since `load_chunks` checks the table against its own manifest.
      - A difference raises a plain `ValueError`, like the reader's other refusals, on one line: "the embeddings in `<interim>/embeddings` are stale: `<field>` is `<saved>` in embeddings_manifest.json but `<current>` in chunks_manifest.json" (or "in the code" for `embedder_version`), one clause per differing field joined by "; ", then "; run uv run python -m thematic_redundancy.representation.build_embeddings again". The values are SHA-256 values, versions or snapshot ids, never text.
      - A missing chunk manifest raises the `FileNotFoundError` of `chunks_manifest`, which already names the chunk build; a damaged one, its `ValueError`. The reader's other checks are unchanged. The docstrings of the module and the reader name the new refusal.

- [x] **T17** Metrics module (silhouette cosine, DB/CH on L2, NPMI, noise %, ARI), tested on synthetic data with known structure.
  - Route D. Risk M. Route declaration (2026-10-19): delegated direct, one writer. Trigger: 2+ non-trivial files: new evaluation modules and their tests, plus this plan.
  - Design: D12 (the metrics) and D28 C4 (the TF-IDF baseline is clustered on its sparse matrix, so the internal metrics accept sparse vectors and densify them only for DB and CH). Pure functions with no I/O; every methodological parameter is an argument with no default, which T18 declares before any run.
  - [x] Follow-up of the approved pre-commit review of the U4 record (`review-1e9733b00298b021`, suggestion): `tests/unit/test_career_affinity.py` says it checks the programs against the order declared in `config/default.yaml`, but it compares them as sets; compare the order.
  - Evidence (2026-10-19). Every value was measured in this unit (M) unless marked D (derived). Synthetic data only; no thesis text was read.
    - Modules, pure and deterministic, with no I/O, under `src/thematic_redundancy/evaluation/`:
      - `clustering_metrics.py`: `internal_metrics(vectors, labels) -> InternalMetrics` (`points`, `noise_points`, `noise_fraction`, `clusters`, `silhouette_cosine`, `davies_bouldin_l2`, `calinski_harabasz_l2`, `undefined_reason`, and the property `clustered_points`); `label_array(labels)`; `NOISE_LABEL` -1, HDBSCAN's noise label. The silhouette uses the cosine on the vectors as given, dense or sparse; DB and CH use the L2-normalized rows, and a sparse input is densified for them only, at points × dimensions × 8 bytes (about 85 MB for the TF-IDF matrix, D28 C4). Noise points are left out of all three, and their count and share of all the points are reported. No sampling.
      - `coherence.py`: `npmi(joint, first, second, units) -> float`, the formula with its limits; `npmi_coherence(topics, reference, *, top_n, unit, window_size) -> CoherenceResult` (`unit`, `window_size`, `top_n`, `units` counted, `topics`, `mean`, `undefined_reason`), each topic a `TopicCoherence` (`terms`, `pair_npmi` in the order of `itertools.combinations`, `npmi`, `absent_terms`, `undefined_reason`).
      - `stability.py`: `seed_stability(labelings, *, noise_handling) -> SeedStability` (`runs`, `noise_handling`, `pairs`, `matrix`, `mean`, `minimum`, `undefined_reason`), each pair a `PairAgreement` (`first`, `second`, `points` compared, `ari`, `undefined_reason`); scikit-learn's ARI.
      - `__init__.py`: the package docstring states the shared conventions.
    - The undefined-metric convention, one for every metric: an invalid input raises `ValueError` (or `TypeError` for a string where segments are expected); a valid input whose metric has no value gives `None` with an `undefined_reason`.
      - Internal metrics, all three together, with the first reason that applies: `no_points` (empty input; `noise_fraction` is then `None` too), `all_noise`, `one_cluster`, `one_point_per_cluster` (scikit-learn defines the three for 2 to n − 1 clusters over n points). Invalid: rows and labels of different lengths, a matrix that is not 2-D, a value that is not finite, a zero row (it has no cosine), labels that are not 1-D integers of at least -1.
      - NPMI: a term in no unit is listed in its topic's `absent_terms`; its pairs are `None`, the topic is `None` (`absent_terms`) and the corpus mean is `None` (`undefined_topics`), so a missing term is never dropped unseen; no topic gives `no_topics`.
      - ARI: a pair with no point left is `None` (`no_points`); the mean and the minimum are `None` with fewer than two runs (`fewer_than_two_runs`) or an undefined pair (`undefined_pairs`).
    - The NPMI conventions:
      - The reference corpus is a sequence of documents, each a sequence of segments (token lists), as a thesis is a sequence of sections (D28 C1); a section becomes a document of its own when passed as a document of one segment. A term of several words joined by single spaces, as the D28 bigrams, occurs where its words appear consecutively inside one segment, never across two.
      - `unit="document"`: each document is one unit, an empty one included. `unit="window"`: windows of `window_size` tokens slide one token at a time over each segment, L − W + 1 windows for a segment of L tokens, one when it is shorter than the window, none when it is empty, never across two segments; a term is in a window when all its words are.
      - No epsilon: a pair that never co-occurs gets -1, the limit as p(a, b) goes to 0; a pair present in every unit gets 1, the limit as p(a, b) = p(a) = p(b) goes to 1.
      - Arguments: `top_n` at least 2, the first `top_n` terms of each topic, none repeated; `window_size` `None` for documents, and at least 2 and at least the words of the longest term for windows.
    - The ARI noise options: `own_label` (noise is one more label, so every point is compared) and `exclude_either` (a point that is noise in either run is left out of that pair). The matrix is symmetric, with each run against itself on the diagonal and `None` where undefined; the mean and the minimum run over the pairs i < j. Two identical partitions give 1 whatever their label ids, by scikit-learn's convention also when both are one cluster or all singletons.
    - Test first:
      - RED, against stubs with the final signatures and `NotImplementedError` bodies: `uv run pytest tests/unit/evaluation -q -p no:cacheprovider` 116 failed, 2 passed in 3.24 s. All 116 failures were `NotImplementedError`; the 2 that passed check that the methodological arguments are keyword-only with no default, which the stubs already declared.

    - Slice: the whole unit was forecast at about 3,300 authored lines (the selection rules and their tests, 1,384 here, M; the orchestration, the records and the command with its dry run, their unit tests, the smoke module and the plan, about 1,950, E), above the limit of about 3,000 set for it. As the parent's rule asks, it stops after the selection rules, complete and verified; the orchestration, the command, the dry-run forecast and the smoke test are the second unit.
    - Module `src/thematic_redundancy/topic_map/selection.py`, standard library only:
      - `summarize(values) -> SeedSummary` (`values`, `mean`, `sd`, `defined`): the mean and the sample SD (ddof 1, E3); one undefined seed leaves both `None`.
      - `beats(a, b, *, higher_is_better)` and `compare_seeded(...)` (`first`, `second` or `tie`): E3's `mean_A − mean_B > max(SD_A, SD_B)`, strict, in the better direction.
      - `choose_k(settings) -> ParameterChoice` (`rule`, `parameter`, `eligible`, `at_bound`, `reason`), over `SettingSummary` (`parameter`, `silhouette`, `noise_fraction`, `metrics_defined`): E2's K*, the highest seed-mean silhouette, an exact tie to the smaller K, and a K* at either end of the sweep flagged `at_bound`, a finding (X2).
      - `choose_min_cluster_size(settings, *, max_mean_noise_fraction)`: among the sizes whose metrics are defined in every seed and whose mean noise share is at most the cap (inclusive), the highest seed-mean silhouette, ties to the lower mean noise share, then to the smaller size; when none passes, `eligible` is false and the size is the lowest-noise one (a tie to the smaller size), as E2 says.
      - `Gates` and `evaluate_gates(candidate, gates) -> GateResult` (`metrics_defined`, `noise_within_cap`, `stable`, `failed`, `passed`): G1 the three internal metrics defined in every seed, G2 the mean noise share at most 0.30, G3 the mean ARI under the primary handling at least 0.65; both thresholds inclusive, as "at most" and "at least" read.
      - `Candidate`: a configuration at its E2 value, with the seed summaries of the silhouette, the noise share, the cluster count and NPMI, the mean ARI, and the sensitivity values (NPMI by windows, the `exclude_either` ARI and, for HDBSCAN, the reassigned-noise silhouette).
      - `stage_one(kmeans, hdbscan, *, gates, fallback) -> Decision` (`first`, `second`, `winner`, `deciding_step`, `deciding_criterion`, `steps`, `findings`), each step a `Comparison` (`step`, `criterion`, `first`, `second`, `outcome`, `reason`) with the values it compared, up to the deciding step: (0) eligibility, so an ineligible HDBSCAN loses (E2); (1) the gates, fewer failures winning, an equal count continuing, every failure a finding; (2) the silhouette and (3) NPMI, by "A beats B"; (4) the higher mean ARI; (5) the lower mean noise share; (6) the fallback algorithm, K-means.
      - `stage_one_sensitivities(...) -> tuple[Sensitivity, ...]` (`name`, `primary`, `value`, `flipped`, `reason`): stage 1 with NPMI by windows of 10 and with the `exclude_either` ARI, and step 2 with the reassigned-noise silhouette; a flip is reported, and the rule is not changed (E3, E4).
      - `stage_two_provisional(first, second, *, gates, fallback_model) -> Decision`: (1) a model whose configuration alone passes every gate wins; (2) the F1, recorded as `skipped` until T22; (3) NPMI by "A beats B"; (4) the fallback model, MiniLM. The decision carries a `provisional` finding (UD3), and `stage_two_sensitivities` gives stage 2 with NPMI by windows. The TF-IDF baseline is refused as a candidate in both stages (UD1).
      - `matched_k(winner)`: the winner's K* when it is K-means, else ⌊mean HDBSCAN cluster count + 0.5⌋; a mean of five integers never ends in .5 (D), so no rounding rule is needed. Refused for the baseline.
      - `criterion_one_npmi(winner, baseline, matched) -> CriterionOne`: the winner beats B0 on NPMI at B0's K* and at the matched K; `met` is `False` when that fails and `None` while the F1 waits for T22. `coefficient_of_variation` (sample SD / |mean|; `None` for a zero or undefined mean) and `criterion_four(winner, *, gates, max_cv) -> CriterionFour`: G3 and both coefficients at most 0.10, inclusive.
      - `representative_seed(matrix, seeds) -> Representative`: the seed run with the highest mean ARI to the other runs, a tie to the seed listed first; an undefined ARI is refused.
      - Every result is a frozen dataclass of plain values, whose `dataclasses.asdict` is JSON data for `selection.json`; every choice and decision carries its reason in words.
    - Implementation notes, for the user's confirmation:
      1. A K whose silhouette is undefined in one seed has no seed mean, so it cannot be K*. D30 does not name the case; K-means on the corpus defines the silhouette for every K of the sweep, so it should not arise (D).
      2. A comparison with an undefined value on either side is a tie; a configuration with an undefined internal metric already fails G1.
      3. Stage 2's first step separates passing every gate from failing one, so two failing configurations tie there, as E3 words it ("a model whose stage-1 configuration passes all the gates wins over one whose configuration does not"); stage 1 counts the failures.

      - `experiment.py`, the core, with no file I/O: `check_declaration(config)` (the SHA-256 of `experiment_declaration()` against the D30 pin `b974c54c…`, held as `DECLARED_SHA256`, and the fallback model against `embedding.models`; `ExperimentError` otherwise); `ExperimentSettings.from_config(config)`, which runs that check first; `ExperimentInputs` (doc codes, the two `SemanticInput` models in the order of `embedding.models`, the sparse TF-IDF matrix, its vocabulary, the full-clean section tokens, the variants and the input-manifest hashes); `run_experiment(inputs, settings, *, ports, cache, timer, report) -> ExperimentResult`; `plan_experiment(...) -> ExperimentPlan` and `ExperimentPlan.counts(matched)`, the dry run's forecast; the row models `RunRow`, `ConfigurationRow`, `TopicRow` and `MapRow`; `Repeatability`; the `ReductionCache` port with `reduction_key` and `NoReductionCache`; `matched_source`, `require_terms_present` and `run_id` (for example `tfidf__kmeans_k24__seed7`). `RUNTIME_FIELDS` names the five columns of `runs.parquet` that a rerun may change.
      - `results_store.py`, the records of E6: `write_outputs(...) -> ExperimentManifest` writes `labels.parquet` and `map.parquet` under `data/processed/<snapshot>/experiments/t19/`, then `runs.parquet`, `configurations.parquet`, `topics.parquet` and `selection.json` under `results/experiments/<snapshot>/t19/`, each atomically, then `manifest.json` last, with the SHA-256 of every other file; `FileReductionCache`, the cache adapter (`umap/<model>__seed<seed>.npy` with a `.json` beside it holding the key, the library versions and the array's SHA-256, written after the array); `library_versions()` and `cpu_record()`.
      - `run_experiment.py`, the command: `load_inputs` (both models through `load_embeddings`, the matrix through `load_tfidf`, the tokens through `load_full_clean_sections`, the doc codes checked equal and in one order, the three input manifests hashed), `documents_by_row`, `read_git_state` (`git rev-parse HEAD` and `git status --porcelain -z --untracked-files=all`, the run's own results directory left out), `describe_plan`, `describe` and `main`.
    - Orchestration, as D30 E1 to E6 and the first slice's design note ask: per (model, seed) one `fit_reduction`, shared through `cluster` by the 22 K-means and 4 HDBSCAN runs (not `build_topic_maps`); the TF-IDF sweep through `kmeans_labels` on the sparse matrix, no UMAP; `internal_metrics` per run on the original vectors (the TF-IDF matrix for B0), noise left out and counted; for HDBSCAN the reassigned-noise silhouette (each noise thesis to the nearest centroid by cosine, the centroid the members' mean through `topic_centroids`); `seed_stability` under `own_label` and `exclude_either` for every configuration row; E2's `choose_k` and `choose_min_cluster_size` (the noise cap from `selection.gates`); the five final configurations C1 to C4 and B0; `ctfidf_topics` and `npmi_coherence` at both settings (documents, and windows of 10) for the final runs only, an absent term stopping the run (`require_terms_present`, which names how many terms, never which); `stage_one` and its sensitivities per model, `stage_two_provisional` and its sensitivities; `matched_k` and the matched runs (none when the matched K is B0's K*, the sweep's five runs when it lies in the sweep, five new runs otherwise); `criterion_one_npmi`, `criterion_four` and `representative_seed`; the representative run's 2-D map with the configured projection (UMAP) and the PCA inspection coordinates, both through `project` with the representative seed; and the repeatability check of E6 (the first (model, seed) reduction refitted and its first K-means rerun, each compared by SHA-256).
    - Resume, as E6 says ("the command resumes per (model, seed)"): each reduction is saved as soon as it is fitted, keyed by the SHA-256 of the model id, its revision, the vectors' bytes, shape and dtype, the UMAP settings and the seed; a later run reuses it only when the key, the library versions (umap-learn, numba, pynndescent, numpy among them) and the array's SHA-256 all match, and records `reduction_reused` true and `umap_seconds` null for its runs.
    - Command: `uv run python -m thematic_redundancy.topic_map.run_experiment [--project-root PATH] [--snapshot-id ID] [--dry-run]`. Order: the configuration, the declaration check (pin and fallback model), the snapshot id, then for `--dry-run` the git state (reported, not refused), the inputs and the forecast; for a real run the git state (a changed or untracked path outside `results/experiments/<snapshot>/t19/` refuses the run, since the manifest records the commit; gitignored paths never count), the inputs, the run, the records and the summary. Progress goes to stderr, the summary (counts, K*, min_cluster_size*, the stage-1 result per model, the provisional winner and its deciding step, the matched K, criteria 1 and 4, the findings count, the repeatability) to stdout, without text. Exit 0 on success, 1 after an error (`OSError`, `ValueError`, YAML), 130 after an interruption with the rerun hint ("the UMAP reductions saved so far are reused by the next run, and records that differ from manifest.json are stale; run the command again").
    - Records, as E6 lists them: `runs.parquet` (one row per clustering run: `run_id`, `config_hash`, the representation, model revision and document vector, the algorithm with `k` or `min_cluster_size`, the seed, points, clusters, noise points and share, the three internal metrics with `undefined_reason`, the reassigned-noise silhouette for HDBSCAN, `labels_sha256`, the `final` and `matched_k` flags, NPMI at both settings for the final runs, and the run-time fields `reduction_reused`, `umap_seconds`, `cluster_seconds`, `metrics_seconds` and `ctfidf_seconds`); `configurations.parquet` (one row per configuration and hyperparameter: the configuration name, the `chosen`, `final` and `matched_k` flags, seed mean and sample SD of the silhouette, DB, CH, noise share, cluster count, reassigned-noise silhouette and NPMI at both settings, and the ARI mean, minimum and matrix, as JSON, under both noise handlings); `topics.parquet` (the final runs' topics, the noise class left out: topic id, size, the top 10 terms with their c-TF-IDF scores, topic NPMI at both settings and `absent_terms`); `selection.json` (`provisional` true, the E2 choices, the five candidates with their gate failures, both stages with every comparison and sensitivity, the winner, the matched K and its source, criteria 1 and 4, the representative run, the map's run, seed and method, and the findings); `manifest.json` (snapshot, `declaration_sha256`, the git commit, the input-manifest SHA-256 values, theses, provisional, winner, matched source, counts, the repeatability check, library versions, CPU, start and end times, seconds, and the SHA-256 of every other file); `data/processed/<snapshot>/experiments/t19/labels.parquet` (`run_id`, `doc_code`, `label`), `map.parquet` (`run_id`, `doc_code`, `label`, `map_x`, `map_y`, `pca_x`, `pca_y`) and `umap/`.
    - Implementation notes, for the user's confirmation:
      1. The worktree could not run the brief's exact dry-run command: its `data/` is a directory junction to the main repository's, and `PathsConfig.resolve_against` refuses a data directory that resolves outside the project root (exit 1, "paths.data_dir resolves to … outside the project root"); that check was not touched. The dry run below ran the worktree's code with the main repository as the project root (its `config/default.yaml` and `config/stopwords_domain_es.txt` are byte-identical to the worktree's, SHA-256 `c7506ed1…` and `b1c9bba9…`), through a wrapper outside the repository that read git from the worktree; nothing was written (no `data/processed/`, and no file under `data/` changed during it). In the main repository the command runs as written.
      2. The dirty-tree check leaves out the run's own results directory, so a rerun after a run is accepted; anything else changed or untracked refuses the run, the plan and the ledger included, so they must be committed first. The dry run only reports it.
      3. A matched K equal to B0's K* adds no run, and criterion 1 then compares the winner with the same B0 runs at both K. A matched K below 2 (an HDBSCAN winner with a mean below 1.5 clusters) would stop the run in `kmeans_labels`; D30 does not name the case, and such a winner would fail G1 (D).
      4. The representative run's topics are its final-run c-TF-IDF in `topics.parquet` (no extra fit); centroids are not saved, since T23 can take them from `labels.parquet` and the vectors by D14's rule. The noise class's terms are not saved.
      5. ARI is computed for every configuration row (148 evaluations on the real grid, D), not only for the final ones, so the stability curve over K is in the records.
      6. The repeatability check is recorded in the manifest and the summary, and does not stop the run when a value differs (X5 asks for it to be reported).
      7. The author-name check of `topics.parquet` (E6) is not automated: it stays the parent's step before the commit, as the plan's next step says.
      8. The cached reductions' file names hold the full model slug; the real repository's longest path is about 195 characters (D), under Windows' 260, but a profiling probe in a deep temporary folder hit the limit ("No such file or directory" on the `.npy.part`), so a run from a very deep path would fail.

### P4 — Labeled set and redundancy (M3–M5)
- [ ] **T20** Pair sampler, plus the Excel workbook generator and importer with validation (O02).
  - Route D. Risk M.
  - To reconcile with the user at T20 (noted 2026-10-13): O02 plans the calibration meeting with 5 pilot pairs, while risk R3 mitigates with a 10-pair pilot. The pilot size is not chosen here. Decided 2026-10-21: 10 pairs (D34 (7)).
  - From D31 (2026-10-20): the labeling guide names the 3 levels and declares their mapping to T22's binary labels before the labeling round (proposal: only the top level counts as redundant); the sampler spreads the pairs evenly over the pairs of annotators, so that each pair of annotators shares about the same number of pairs for the Cohen's κ diagnostic.
  - Design: D34 (all nine points), D31, D24 and O09, D28 C10, O02 and R3. Three units, each a commit, in this order:
    1. **T20a, the sampler.** A `labeling` configuration block that declares D34's values and a seed before any draw; a module that builds the universe, the four pools with their strata, the pilot and the labeled set from the three representations' document vectors and the fichas; a command in the style of T24a's that writes the draw's records: the drawn pairs (doc codes, pool, stratum, the three scores and ranks, pilot or not) under `data/labels/` (gitignored), and an aggregate summary with no doc code under `results/` (D33's rule). The real draw runs at the commit of this unit, as T19 and T24a did. Route D: delegated direct, one bounded writer; trigger: the long-session backstop (the parent's context).
    2. **T20b, the workbooks.** The name forms and ORCIDs as a public set for the guard (D34 (1)); the pilot workbook; the assignment of the labeled pairs to the configured number of annotators, spread evenly over the pairs of annotators; the main workbooks, one per annotator, whose guide shows the pilot's agreed labels; randomized pair order and sides; no score, link or person. It waits for nothing but T20a; the main workbooks are generated after the user confirms the number of annotators and loads the pilot's agreed labels.
    3. **T20c, the importer.** The validation of each returned workbook (an incomplete one is refused, T21 N4; the `zlib.error` and `EOFError` gap of T10c is closed for this loader); the labels with their annotator (`labels.parquet`), the 3-level and binary labels, Fleiss' κ inputs; the adjudication workbook for the disputed pairs and its import (D34 (9)).
  - Evidence, T20a (2026-10-21), the sampler, uncommitted at `925600a`; D34 (4) to (7) covered, and the pairs' records that (1), (2), (8) and (9) need in T20b and T20c (pair id, phase, doc codes, programs, stratum, pool); the real draw is left for the commit of this unit:

  - Implementation notes for the user to confirm (T20a):
    1. Allocation with a floor (D34 (6)): each stratum with candidates first gets the floor, or all it has when fewer; the rest of the quota goes by largest remainder in proportion to each stratum's candidates beyond the floor (not to all its candidates), which never exceeds a stratum's candidates. On MiniLM's real top 1 % (13, 1,909, 8, 198, 272, 364) it gives 2, 7, 2, 3, 3, 3.
    2. Tie rules: strata in the configured program order (sistemas, industrial, electronica, mecanica, minas), then `between`, and a tie of remainders goes to the earlier one; a tie of scores at the top's cut goes to the earlier pair; the pilot's pools tie in draw order, so 10 pairs give SBERT-high 3, TF-IDF-high 2, mid-range 3 (before random at the same remainder 0.5) and random 2; SBERT-high's 3 split by largest remainder of equal sizes, so MiniLM 2 and mpnet 1 (the earlier of `embedding.models`).
    3. Exactness: the top count is `ceil` of the share read as a decimal (0.01 × 276,396 gives 2,764), and the mid band compares twice the average ranks, whole numbers, with the bounds read as decimals, so a pair at exactly 0.90 is in and one at exactly 0.99 out.
    4. Order and seeds: the theses in doc-code order and the pairs row-major; every sample is `random.Random("<seed>:<phase>:<pool>[:<model>][:<stratum>]").sample` over the candidates left, in that order, so no input order changes the draw. The pilot draws from the same candidate sets as the labeled set (each model's top, TF-IDF's top, the mid-range, the universe), unstratified, and the labeled set from what the pilot left.
    5. Pair id: `P-` (pilot) or `L-` (labeled set) and the first 8 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the two doc codes, sorted and joined by `:`; it tells neither the pool nor the draw order, so T20b may show it as the blind pair code. A collision among the drawn pairs would stop the draw.
    6. A pool with fewer candidates left than its quota stops the draw instead of drawing fewer; on the real data the smallest is mpnet's top with 2,764.
    7. Locations: a new key `labeling.records_dir` (`data/labels/labeled_set`), refused outside `paths.data_dir` as the workbook folder; the pairs go to `<records_dir>/<snapshot>/pairs.parquet`, the aggregates to `results/labeled_set/<snapshot>/draw/`, the `draw/` folder leaving room for T20b's and T20c's records. `pilot_floor` (2) is a key of its own, from D34 (7)'s "2 or 3 per pool"; the configuration also refuses `mid_low` not below `mid_high`, a `pilot_size` below 4 × `pilot_floor`, and a `stratum_floor` × 6 strata above the smallest stratified pool.
    8. One-time draw: either record of an earlier draw, the gitignored `pairs.parquet` or the tracked `manifest.json`, refuses a real run, checked before the lock and again inside it; an interrupted draw's partial records must be moved away by hand. The dry run says when a draw exists.
    9. Privacy: the gap run's `check_privacy`, imported from `gaps/results_store.py`, over the snapshot's strings and the 744 doc codes; its message names titles, abstracts, names and ORCID iDs, and a doc code is reported in the same words.
    10. Records: `pairs.parquet` holds the three scores and ranks as lists in the order of its `representations` column (the model ids, then `tfidf`), the mean rank, both programs and `known_duplicate`; `ranges.parquet` holds a `mean` row per pool; the manifest's `versions` are T19's `library_versions()` as they are, and its `inputs` the three input manifests, `metadata.jsonl` and `default.yaml`. On the real data the random pool draws 23 of its 30 pairs between programs, as 69.7 % of the universe crosses programs (D).

  - Implementation notes for the user to confirm (T20b):
    1. Name guard (D34 (1)): the public functions live beside the privacy guard in `corpus/profile.py`, so T10b's redactor and the guard read the same forms. Every T20 text first goes through T10b's redactor, unchanged (a name standing as whole words becomes `[nombre omitido]`), then through the guard without word boundaries over the NFC, case-folded, whitespace-collapsed text, so a name glued to another word stops the step. The guard reads cells, sheet names and drop-down texts before anything is written, and names the place (workbook, sheet, cell, pair code), never the name.
    2. Blind code (D34 (2)): two letters of `GHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ` and three digits of `23456789`, such as `KT482` (131,072 codes), from `secrets`, drawn again on a collision; no vowel, no letter like a digit and no hexadecimal letter, so no SHA-256 in a manifest can hold a code by chance. Made once, at the `pilot` step, for all 130 drawn pairs, and kept only in `blind_codes.parquet`; a pilot run that stopped before its manifest keeps the codes it wrote. Every manifest's privacy guard includes the codes, the pair ids and the doc codes.
    3. Layout: a pair is a block of four rows (`Título`, `Resumen`, `Objetivos`, `Palabras clave`) with `Tesis A` and `Tesis B` side by side (70 characters wide each, wrapped, the title in bold); `N.º`, `Código`, the label and the note span the block; panes frozen at `D2`; the label is a drop-down that refuses other values. The pilot's columns `Etiqueta acordada` and `Justificación`; an annotator's `Etiqueta` and `Notas (opcional)`; the examples sheet uses the pilot's columns without a drop-down. Sheets `Guía`, (`Ejemplos`), `Etiquetado`; the last opens first, as in T10b.
    4. Order and sides, seeded and recorded, from `labeling.seed` with no new seed key: a workbook's pairs sorted by pair id and shuffled with `random.Random("<seed>:order:<workbook>")` (`pilot`, `annotator-<k>`); a pair's theses swap when `random.Random("<seed>:sides:<pair id>").random() < 0.5`, the same in every workbook. Recorded in `pilot_layout.parquet` and `main_layout.parquet` (the doc code shown on each side and the `swapped` flag).
    5. A ficha without objectives: the `Objetivos` row shows `(La ficha no registra objetivos.)`, and likewise `(La ficha no registra resumen.)` and `(La ficha no registra palabras clave.)`; the guide explains them; keywords are joined by `; `. On the real data 16 included fichas lack objectives (D34's "16 of metadata only"), none an abstract or keywords; the pilot shows none of them, the labeled set 5.
    6. The scale's wording: D34 (3) gives no definition for "No relacionado"; the guide says "Distinto tema." It states the binary mapping (only "Redundante" counts as redundant, D31 (b)).
    7. Assignment (D34 (8)): pairs of annotators in `itertools.combinations` order; the labeled set taken pool by pool in D34 (5)'s order and SBERT-high model by model, each sorted by pair id and shuffled with `random.Random("<seed>:assignment:<pool>[:<model>]")`, then dealt in turn with the count continuing across pools: exactly 40 per pair of annotators with 3 annotators or 20 with 4, each pool and model within one pair. `labeling.annotators` is a required key whose value may be `null`, kept out of the draw's manifest and summary so T20a's records stay identical.
    8. Main workbooks: no justification column, an optional `Notas (opcional)` column instead, which helps the adjudication (T20c) and costs nothing when empty. The `Ejemplos` sheet shows the 10 pilot pairs in the pilot's order and sides with their agreed labels and justifications. A pair keeps its code and sides in both annotators' workbooks.
    9. Pilot import: labels are read like T10b's verdicts (case and spacing ignored); a label must stand in the first row of its pair's block; the justification is optional; at least one pair of each level is required (D34 (7)). The reader catches T10b's unreadable-file errors plus `zlib.error` and `EOFError` (`workbook.UNREADABLE`); T10b's own loader is untouched, so T10c's first item stays open for T10b.
    10. Steps and records: each step happens once; a real run refuses a dirty tree outside `results/labeled_set/<snapshot>/workbooks/` and its own earlier records (checked before the lock and inside it), holds that directory's lock, writes its gitignored records, then its tracked manifest last (`pilot_manifest.json`, `pilot_labels_manifest.json`, `main_manifest.json`: counts, aggregates and the SHA-256 of the draw's manifest, `pairs.parquet`, the fichas manifest, the metadata, `default.yaml`, the earlier steps' manifests, the filled pilot workbook and every record written). A failure after some records are written names them to move away. Every step reads `pairs.parquet` only if it matches the SHA-256 that the draw's committed manifest records. `redacted_cells` in the main manifest counts cells over all workbooks (a pair in two workbooks counts twice).
    11. For T20c: each annotator's workbook is read with `workbook.read_labels(path, MAIN_LAYOUT, {row.code: row.n for row in main_layout if row.annotator == k})` (blank labels are already refused, T21 N4); `assignment.parquet` gives each pair's two annotators; `LEVELS` and `BINARY` give the 3-level and binary labels; the adjudication workbook can be built with `build_workbook` and `SheetContent` and checked with `guard_workbooks`; a new step is one more `Step` in `pair_workbooks.STEPS`.
    12. T20a's follow-up (3): `synthetic_files.build_representations` runs the real embeddings and TF-IDF builds with stand-in models; the TF-IDF build's count check is patched out during that setup only, since the synthetic counts are not declared ahead.

  - What is built, `src/thematic_redundancy/evaluation/agreement.py` (228 lines, M):
    - `cohen_kappa(first, second, *, categories, weights) -> CohenKappa`:
      - Cohen's κ for two annotators. `weights` is one of `"none"` (nominal), `"linear"` or `"quadratic"`, the last two being weighted κ for an ordered scale; the disagreement weights are `|i - j| / (k - 1)` or their square.
      - The result holds the contingency table (rows: first annotator; columns: second), the observed and expected agreement, κ, and `undefined_reason`.
      - Undefined cases: no item is `"no_items"`; both annotators giving every item one same category is `"expected_agreement_is_one"` (p_e = 1, so κ is 0/0), detected on integer counts, not by comparing floats. Both annotators each constant, on different categories, is a defined κ = 0.
      - `categories` is explicit and ordered: an unused category is an empty row and column, and the order matters only to the weighted κ.
    - `consensus_labels(pair_ids, first, second, *, categories) -> Consensus`: `agreed` holds the pairs where the labels match (`AgreedLabel`) and `needs_adjudication` the others, with both labels (`Disagreement`). Both lists keep the input order. There is no automatic tie-breaking: the plan defines none, and R3 plans the re-labeling of disputed pairs.
    - Inputs are validated with explicit `ValueError`s: lengths must match; every label must be one of the categories; categories must not repeat, there must be at least two, and each must be a string or a non-boolean integer; unknown weights are rejected; pair ids must be non-empty strings with no repeats.
  - Implementation notes for the user to confirm:
    - N1. Which κ is checked against D15's target of κ ≥ 0.6. D15 says "3-level scale" without naming the levels. If the scale is ordinal, the weighted κ (linear or quadratic) applies. All three variants are computed. Proposal: report the unweighted κ as the one checked against the target, since D15 says "Cohen's κ", and the weighted κ as a supplement. Declare the choice before the labeling round. **Decided 2026-10-20 (D31):** Fleiss' κ on the binary labels T22 uses is checked against the target; Fleiss' κ on the 3 levels is reported as a complement.
    - N2. Rotating annotators. U3 plans 3–4 advisors with about 80 pairs each and 2 annotators per pair. That is about 240 labels for about 120 pairs (D), so the two annotators of a pair change from pair to pair, while Cohen's κ assumes the same two annotators over every item. Options: (a) κ per pair of annotators, over the pairs they share; (b) κ over "first slot vs second slot", which pools different annotators into each slot; (c) Fleiss' κ, which allows a fixed number of raters per item drawn from a pool (not built here; statsmodels 0.15.0 ships `fleiss_kappa`). Decide at T20, when the pairs are assigned. **Decided 2026-10-20 (D31):** (c), Fleiss' κ over all the pairs, with (a), Cohen's κ per pair of annotators, as a diagnostic only.
    - N3. Adjudication. Disputed pairs go to adjudication and nothing is tie-broken. Not built: who adjudicates (a third annotator, or the pair in discussion), and a helper that merges the adjudicated labels into the final labels for T22.
    - N4. Missing labels. Every pair must carry one category from each annotator, so the T20 importer must reject or flag an incomplete workbook before κ.
    - N5. No interval for κ. No standard error or confidence interval is computed. statsmodels' `cohens_kappa` gives the asymptotic one if the thesis wants it.

N16. `evaluation/__init__.py` was not edited; its docstring already names kappa and Likert analysis.

In all 1,651 lines (835 of source, 816 of tests) and 65,835 B (D). SHA-256, first 12 hex characters (M): `agreement.py` `a3cf73ff3891`, `aiken.py` `8490b81bcccc`, `likert.py` `d35566d4f666`.
  - Fleiss' κ, added 2026-10-20 for D31 (route: inline, in the main tree):
    - `fleiss_kappa(ratings, *, categories) -> FleissKappa` in `evaluation/agreement.py`: one sequence of labels per item, every item with the same number m ≥ 2 of ratings, from raters who may change from item to item. `(P − P_e) / (1 − P_e)`, where `P` is the mean over the items of the share of agreeing pairs among an item's ratings and `P_e` the sum of the squared category shares over all the ratings (Fleiss, 1971). The result holds the counts (items × categories), the category shares, `P`, `P_e`, κ and `undefined_reason` (`"no_items"`; `"expected_agreement_is_one"` when every rating falls in one category, checked on integer counts). It rejects with `ValueError` an item given as a string, a first item with fewer than two ratings, an item whose number of ratings differs from the first's, and a label outside the categories.
    - Oracles (in `test_rater_agreement.py`): the Wikipedia worked example and the diagnoses of Fleiss (1971) as statsmodels 0.15.0's `test_inter_rater.py` holds them (κ 0.210, and R's `irr::kappam.fleiss` 0.4302445, matched to 5e-8); the hand formula in exact fractions; statsmodels' `fleiss_kappa(table, method="fleiss")` on 120 random correlated items with 2, 3 and 5 ratings each (rel 1e-12); and Scott's pi by hand for two ratings per item, which differs from Cohen's κ on the same table (SAS table: pooled margins 5, 21, 12 of 38).
    - RED against a stub raising `NotImplementedError`: 18 failed, 40 passed (M). GREEN: 58 passed in 2.57 s (M). 18 new tests (M).
    - Mutants: 6 throwaway mutants, 6 killed, the module restored byte-identical after each (SHA-256 checked) (M): F1 m² instead of m(m − 1); F2 the chance agreement as the largest share instead of the sum of squares; F3 the undefined case not detected; F4 items with different numbers of ratings accepted; F5 n² instead of n(n − 1) per category; F6 a string item accepted.
    - Cohen's κ per pair of annotators needs each label's annotator, which the T20 import provides; the caller passes each pair of annotators' shared items to `cohen_kappa`.
- [x] **T22** Redundancy module: cosine search, τ from the PR curve via 5-fold CV, a bootstrap CI, and the SBERT vs TF-IDF comparison.
  - Route D. Risk M.
  - O09 (decided 2026-10-19): flag the pair `DOC-1b29cde4` / `DOC-b2303c69` as a known true duplicate, a re-deposit in the repository, and mention it in the results.
  - Route declaration, 2026-10-19 overnight: delegated direct, one writer in an isolated worktree (detached at `a5930ca`); trigger: parallel units launched together. Built and tested on synthetic data only; the real run needs the labeled set of T21.
  - Design. Four pure modules under `src/thematic_redundancy/redundancy/`, in the house style of `evaluation/` (typed frozen results, explicit errors, each methodological value an argument with no default):
    - `search.py`, the cosine search. Every row and the query are L2-normalized in float64 (dense rows through `representation.vectors.l2_normalize`; sparse rows through scikit-learn's `normalize`), so the score is the cosine whatever the vectors' length or dtype; a row or a query with no direction is refused. Rows with equal scores keep the order of their index. `exclude` names the query's own row when the query is a corpus document; an identical copy in another row is still found. A `k` larger than the rows returns them all.
    - `threshold.py`, F1 and τ. A pair is predicted redundant when its score is at least τ (`score >= tau`), the rule of every point of scikit-learn's `precision_recall_curve`, so equal scores always fall on the same side of τ. F1 = 2·TP / (2·TP + FP + FN), undefined (`None`) only with no positive label and no positive prediction. The max-F1 point takes the distinct scores as candidate thresholds. Cross-validation: the folds come from scikit-learn's `StratifiedKFold` or `KFold`, `shuffle=True`, `random_state=seed`, and depend on the labels and the seed only, so two representations over the same pairs share them; each fold's τ is the max-F1 point of its training pairs, and its held-out pairs are counted at that τ; the CV F1 is the F1 of the held-out counts summed over the folds (each pair held out once), with the mean of the folds' F1 beside it; the final τ is chosen by `final_tau`, and the counts and F1 of all the pairs at it are the in-sample values that the bootstrap resamples. A training split with no positive pair stops the calibration.
    - `comparison.py`, the bootstrap and the comparison (D30 E3). Resamples: `numpy.random.default_rng(seed).integers(0, pairs, size=(resamples, pairs))`, which depend on the number of pairs and the settings only, so every interval with the same settings reads the same resamples. The F1 interval recomputes F1 at the fixed τ in each resample and takes the percentile interval (numpy's default linear quantiles). The paired ΔF1 = F1(first) − F1(second) uses the same drawn pairs for both, each at its own fixed CV-selected τ. Verdict: lower bound above 0, `first_higher`; upper bound below 0, `second_higher`; an interval that holds or touches 0, `tie`. Resamples whose F1 (or either F1, for ΔF1) is undefined are left out and counted. `compare_representations` calibrates each τ with the same CV settings, then bootstraps each F1 and ΔF1 with the same settings; `first_beats_second` is acceptance criterion 1's F1 part when the semantic winner is the first and TF-IDF the second.
    - `known_duplicates.py` (O09): flags the pairs that join the two documents of a listed duplicate, in either order. The list is the new `redundancy.known_duplicates` configuration key, holding `[DOC-1b29cde4, DOC-b2303c69]`; no thesis text is involved, and the pair is not excluded.
  - Public API: `search`: `Neighbor(index, score)`, `cosine_scores(documents, query)`, `cosine_top_k(documents, query, *, k, exclude)`, `nearest_documents(documents, index, *, k)`, `pair_cosines(documents, pairs)`. `threshold`: `ConfusionCounts` (with `precision`, `recall`, `f1`), `CurvePoint`, `MaxF1Threshold`, `CvSettings(folds, seed, stratified, f1_tie, final_tau)`, `FoldOutcome`, `ThresholdCalibration`, `confusion_at(scores, labels, *, tau)`, `max_f1_threshold(scores, labels, *, f1_tie)`, `calibrate_threshold(scores, labels, settings)`, `labeled_pairs(scores, labels)`; the literals `F1Tie` (`highest_tau`, `lowest_tau`) and `FinalTau` (`all_pairs`, `fold_mean`). `comparison`: `BootstrapSettings(resamples, seed, interval)`, `F1Interval`, `F1Difference`, `RepresentationComparison` (with `first_beats_second`), `resample_indices(pairs, settings)`, `bootstrap_f1(scores, labels, *, tau, settings)`, `paired_f1_difference(first_scores, second_scores, labels, *, first_tau, second_tau, settings)`, `compare_representations(first_scores, second_scores, labels, *, names, cv, bootstrap)`. `known_duplicates`: `known_duplicate_flags(pairs, known)`. Configuration: `RedundancyConfig.known_duplicates`, a tuple of pairs of `DOC-` codes (8 lowercase hex digits), two different codes per pair, each pair once in either order; it is outside the experiment declaration, whose pinned hash is unchanged.
  - Implementation notes for the user to confirm (the plan does not fix these; none is in the configuration yet, so the T22 run command cannot start until they are declared, for example as `redundancy` keys):
    1. The rule at τ is `score >= tau`, taken from the PR curve that D15 and D30 name (scikit-learn's convention); pairs with equal scores are never split. τ is a score of the training pairs, not a midpoint between two scores.
    2. `f1_tie`: when several thresholds share the highest F1, `highest_tau` (fewer pairs flagged) or `lowest_tau` (more pairs found). Proposal: `highest_tau`.
    3. `final_tau`: `all_pairs` (the max-F1 point of all the labeled pairs, the usual refit after cross-validation; its in-sample F1 is optimistic, which the CV F1 corrects) or `fold_mean` (the mean of the 5 fold τ; it may not be a score). Proposal: `all_pairs`.
    4. `stratified`: stratified folds keep the share of positive pairs in each fold; with about 120 pairs (D15), plain folds may leave a fold with few positives. Proposal: `true`.
    5. The fold seed: proposal 7, the D30 bootstrap seed. With fewer positive pairs than folds, scikit-learn warns, and a training split without a positive stops the calibration.
    6. The CV F1 pools the held-out counts of the 5 folds; the mean of the folds' F1 is reported beside it, and is undefined when a fold holds no positive and predicts none. Pooling for F1 is the recommendation usually attributed to Forman and Scholz (2010, SIGKDD Explorations 12(1)); to be verified before citing.
    7. Acceptance criterion 2 ("F1 ≥ 0.70 at the selected τ, cross-validated and reported with a bootstrap CI") does not say which F1 is compared with 0.70: the CV F1 (held out) or the F1 of all the pairs at the final τ (the one whose bootstrap CI D30 declares). Proposal: judge on the CV F1 and report both, with the CI of the in-sample F1. The 0.70 itself is not in the configuration.
    8. The bootstrap keeps each τ fixed (D30), so its interval leaves out the variability of choosing τ; a stated limitation. Resamples with an undefined F1 are left out and counted, and the count is reported. The percentile bounds use numpy's default linear interpolation.
    9. The verdict follows the side of the interval, not the sign of the observed ΔF1 (they can disagree only in pathological cases); an interval that touches 0 is a tie.
    10. The labels are binary (1 = redundant). D15's 3-level scale must be mapped to them in T20 or T21 (for example, only the top level as redundant); T22 does not decide it.
    11. The scores of the labeled pairs come from `pair_cosines` over the `section_mean` vectors of each representation (`embedding.document_vector`, `tfidf.document_vector`), as D30 E1 uses them.
    12. O09: the results must mention the pair `DOC-1b29cde4` / `DOC-b2303c69` as a known true duplicate wherever it appears (search results, labeled pairs); the flag only marks it.
