The four calibration values that T22 left undeclared (its notes 2 to 5) are new required `redundancy` keys, declared as T22 proposed: `cv_seed: 7`, `cv_stratified: true`, `f1_tie: highest_tau`, `final_tau: all_pairs`; outside the experiment declaration, whose SHA-256 is unchanged. `cv_folds` (5) and `experiment.selection.f1` (2,000, 7, 0.95) are reused as declared.
    2. Criterion 2's 0.70 is the constant `CRITERION_TWO_MIN_F1` (the acceptance criterion states it, as T24a keeps D32's text values as constants), judged on the cross-validated F1 as T22's note 7 proposes, with the F1 at τ and its interval beside it, for the winner and for each representation.
    3. The contract lives in `redundancy/labels.py`; `ConsensusLabel` enforces the domains and the label rule when T20c builds its rows. An "extra pair" is an id that is not of the labeled set; an extra column is refused by the table schema.
    4. The score check is exact (`np.array_equal`), since the real probe found the recomputed cosines bit-identical to T20a's.
    5. A run happens once, as T20a's draw: an earlier `manifest.json` or the gitignored `pair_scores.parquet` refuses a real run, checked before the lock and again inside it; a partial run's records must be moved away by hand, as the refusal says.
    6. `--labels PATH` is accepted with `--dry-run` only; a real run refuses it before reading anything, so tracked records always come from the contract's file.
    7. Stage 2 reuses T19's `stage_two_provisional` for steps 1, 3 and 4 and puts the F1 step in place of the skipped one; T19's provisional decision is recomputed from T19's recorded candidates and their recorded gate failures, and must equal `selection.json`'s, or the run stops.
    8. An undefined F1 step (no interval; an interval whose side is not the observed higher F1) writes the run's records with exit 0, `stage_two: undefined`, no winner, and a finding and a consequence naming the decision for the user; neither case is expected with real labels.
    9. Criterion 1: the F1 part is the stage-2 winner against TF-IDF (`first_beats_second`), each model's part always reported; the NPMI part is copied from T19 only when the winner is confirmed, and is pending when it changes (T19 holds no NPMI of B0 at the new matched K) or is undefined.
    10. A τ that cannot be calibrated (no redundant pair, or so few that a training split holds none, such as 1) stops the run with the reason; scikit-learn's warning about the least populated class reaches stderr.
    11. `curves.parquet`, the precision-recall curve of all the labeled pairs per representation with the selected τ flagged, was added for the thesis's figure; it holds counts and thresholds only.
    12. The input checks: every input of T20a's manifest but `default.yaml` (which gains these keys) and every input of T19's manifest must equal the loaded ones; T19's declaration must be the configuration's.
    13. stdout is ASCII (`tau`, `dF1`) for the cp1252 console; `summary.md` and `selection.json` are UTF-8 with τ and ΔF1.
    14. T19's records keep `provisional: true` and are never rewritten; T22's `selection.json` is the record that closes stage 2.

  - Route D. Risk M.

Files: two new modules (`topic_map/assignment.py`, `redundancy/saturation.py`) and their unit tests. No configuration change, no `__init__.py` change, no change to an existing module.
  - Design: D14 (nearest centroid, cosine, original space, no re-clustering, one rule for K-means and HDBSCAN), O06 (the atypical flag), D13 and the initial configuration's `saturation` row ("quartiles of topic size"), D30 E5 and T16 note 2 (a centroid is the plain mean of its members' original vectors; noise has none), and the plan's layout (`topic_map/ # ..., assignment`; `redundancy/ # similarity search, threshold calibration, saturation`).
  - Evidence (2026-10-19). Every value was measured in this unit (M) unless marked D (derived). Synthetic data only.
    - Modules:
      - `topic_map/assignment.py`: `TopicCentroids` (`topics`, `centroids`, `dimensions`; `from_topic_map(topic_map)`), the map's topic labels, strictly ascending and at least 0, with a read-only float64 copy of its original-space centroids; `assign_proposal(vector, centroids) -> TopicAssignment` (`topic`, `similarity`, `runner_up`, `runner_up_similarity`, `margin`) and `assign_proposals(vectors, centroids)`, one assignment per row; `member_similarities(vectors, labels, centroids)`, each clustered thesis's cosine to its own topic's centroid, noise left out; `atypical_threshold(vectors, labels, centroids, *, percentile, method, scope) -> AtypicalThreshold` (`percentile`, `method`, `scope`, `topics`, `members`, `values`; `value_for(topic)`); `check_atypical(assignment, threshold) -> AtypicalCheck` (`topic`, `similarity`, `threshold`, `atypical`); the constants `ATYPICAL_PERCENTILE` (5.0, O06), `PERCENTILE_METHODS` (numpy's 13 methods), `THRESHOLD_SCOPES` (`global`, `per_topic`) and `CENTROID_DIRECTION_TOLERANCE` (1e-6). Errors are `TopicMapError`.
      - `redundancy/saturation.py`: `saturation_quartiles(labels, *, method, boundary) -> SaturationQuartiles` (`method`, `boundary`, `cuts`, `topics`, `undefined_reason`; `of_topic(topic)`), each topic a `TopicSaturation` (`topic`, `size`, `quartile` 1 to 4, and the property `level`, `"Q1"` to `"Q4"`); `QUARTILE_CUTS` (25, 50, 75) and `QUARTILE_BOUNDARIES` (`lower`, `upper`). Errors are `ValueError`, since `redundancy/__init__.py` has no error class and was outside this unit.
    - Conventions:
      - Assignment: the cosine to every centroid; the highest wins; an exact tie (float equality, no tolerance) goes to the lower topic label; the runner-up is the next topic in the same order, so a tie's runner-up is the other tied topic with margin 0; with one topic there is no runner-up and no margin. Noise has no centroid, so it is never a target, and `TopicCentroids` refuses a label below 0.
      - Every cosine, a member's or a proposal's, is computed one vector at a time by the same steps (unit vector, then the unit centroids times it, clipped to [-1, 1]), so a thesis submitted as a proposal gets exactly its member similarity, and a batch gives what each proposal gives alone (both tested).
      - The threshold: numpy's `percentile` over the member similarities, all clustered theses pooled (`global`) or each topic's members (`per_topic`); a proposal is atypical when its similarity is strictly below the threshold of its assigned topic.
      - Guards: the labels, the vectors and the centroids must belong together: equal lengths and dimensions, the same topics (each with members), no zero member vector, and each centroid pointing where the mean of its members' vectors points, within 1e-6 per unit-vector component (L2-normalized centroids are accepted; another labeling or row order is refused). T16's `topic_centroids` is reused for that check.
      - Saturation: a topic's size is its member count from the labels, noise excluded; the cuts are numpy's `percentile` at 25, 50 and 75 of the sizes, one value per topic; each topic's quartile is by value, so equal sizes share a quartile; with no topic, or all topics of one size, every quartile is `None` with `undefined_reason` `no_topics` or `equal_sizes`. It never flags anything (D13).
      - No result holds a text; no file is read or written; importing the two modules imports none of BERTopic, UMAP and hdbscan (M, a fresh process).
    - Implementation notes for the user to confirm (the T14 and T16 precedent). The plan does not fix these, so each is an argument with no default, or a documented behavior:
      1. The threshold's scope, `scope` with no default. O06 reads "the P5 of member-to-own-centroid similarities", one pool, without saying global or per topic. Recommended: `"global"`, the plain reading of O06; a per-topic P5 on small topics lies near the topic's minimum (with 5 members, numpy's linear P5 is the smallest value plus 0.2 of the gap to the second, D: 0.05 × 4 = 0.2), and K-means can give topics of a few theses.
      2. The percentile method, `method` with no default. Recommended: `"linear"`, numpy's own default (Hyndman and Fan's type 7). With 744 members, `linear` puts the P5 at 0-based position 37.15 of the sorted similarities (D: 0.05 × 743), and numpy's 13 methods put it between positions 36.0 (`closest_observation`) and 38.0 (`higher`) (M, on `arange(744)`), so they differ only in how they pick or interpolate among the 37th to 39th smallest similarities.
      3. In-sample centroids: each member's similarity is to its topic's centroid, which includes that member (the plan's centroid, T16 note 2), while a proposal belongs to no centroid. Member similarities are therefore slightly higher than leave-one-out ones, and the flag slightly more liberal, more so for small topics. No leave-one-out variant was built, since the plan does not ask for one.
      4. "Own" centroid, as O06 says, not the nearest one: a thesis that K-means placed in a topic (in the UMAP space) can lie nearer another centroid in the original space; its similarity to its own centroid enters the pool (tested).
      5. The saturation cut method, `method` with no default, and where a size equal to a cut goes, `boundary` with no default. Recommended: `"linear"`, and `"lower"` (quartile k holds the sizes above cut k-1 and up to cut k, the right-closed bins of pandas' `qcut`), under which the smallest topic is always in Q1, which gap rule (a)'s "bottom quartile of topic size" reads. Its cost: when the largest topics tie on the top cut they stay in Q3 (sizes 5, 6, 7, 9, 9, 9, 9, 9 give Q1, Q1, Q2 and five Q2s, tested). `"upper"` always puts the largest topic in Q4 but can leave Q1 empty (sizes 5, 5, 5, 5, 6, 7, 8, 20 give four Q2s, tested).
      6. The quartiles are over the topic sizes, one value per topic, not weighted by theses; the plan's "quartiles of topic size" is read that way.
      7. No configuration block was added: O06's 5 is passed as `ATYPICAL_PERCENTILE`, and the scope, the two methods and the boundary are for the callers (T26, T27, T28) to pass once confirmed; a `proposal` block in `config/default.yaml` could hold them then. T24 could reuse `saturation_quartiles` for gap rule (a), so that saturation and the gap rule share one cut; T24 decides.
      8. The package docstring of `topic_map/__init__.py` lists the T16 modules and not `assignment`; it was left untouched, since no `__init__.py` was in this unit's surface.

The declared values come from the configuration through `GapSettings`.
    - Implementation notes for the user to confirm:
      1. D16 says "five types", while the plan lists only a–d (and the scope says "five types plus a stability filter"). Only a–d were built; no fifth rule was invented. Possibly the fifth is the "never touched" type against OpenAlex (D17, T25); this was not verified, since the thesis plan PDF was not read in this unit.
      2. Rule (a), the bottom quartile: a topic is in it when its size is at most the first quartile of the map's topic sizes, by linear interpolation (`numpy.quantile`'s default), so every topic tied at the quartile is included; the noise (-1) is no topic and is left out of the sizes.
      3. Rule (a), the NPMI floor: not declared in the plan, so `npmi_floor` is a required argument; its value, and which NPMI setting it reads (D30 E4's primary `document` unit is the natural one), must be declared before T24 runs on real data. A topic with an undefined NPMI never reaches the floor.
      4. Rule (b), "each topic's 3 nearest topics": the cosine between the T16 centroids (means of the members' original vectors, D30 E5), the 3 others with the highest cosine, a tie to the lower topic label; a pair is a candidate when either topic is among the other's nearest (the union, not mutual neighbors). The pair records its better rank.
      5. Rule (b), "bridge document" (the plan does not define it): a thesis, noise included, whose two most similar centroids (cosine on the original vectors) are the pair and that is more similar to the pair's midpoint direction (the normalized sum of the two unit centroids) than to either centroid, that is, it lies in the middle half of the arc between them, in the plane of the two centroids. It has no parameter. Alternatives that need a value: a margin on the difference of the two similarities; or a thesis assigned to one topic whose nearest centroid is the other's. Rule (b) therefore also takes the original document vectors, which the map does not hold.
      6. Rule (c), "present" and "absent or rare": a topic is present in the source with at least `present_min_count` theses and a share of at least `present_min_share`, and absent or rare in the target with a share of at most `rare_max_share` (0 means strictly absent); `rare_max_share` must be below `present_min_share`. The values must be declared.
      7. Rule (c), which scores count as affine: a score of at least `min_affinity`, which must be 1 (partial or high) or 2 (high only) on the U4 scale; 0 is refused, since it means "no affinity". The value must be declared.
      8. Rule (c) and R5: shares are per program, the topic's theses of a program over all that program's theses in the map, noise included, so Industrial's 46% of the corpus does not dominate. A program with no thesis in the map is neither source nor target. Rules (a), (b) and (d) are corpus-level; the gaps of (a) and (d) carry their per-program counts, for R5's "report per program". A per-program variant of rule (d) was not built.
      9. Rule (c) and D29: a program in `gap_target_excluded` (Electrónica) is never a target and can be a source; tested on the committed file.
      10. Rule (d): the windows are `snapshot.year_start` to `gaps.temporal_split_year` − 1 and `gaps.temporal_split_year` to `snapshot.year_end` (2021–2023 and 2024–2026); a share is the topic's theses of a window over all the window's theses, noise included; the direction is a decline only (earlier share minus later share); "minimum count 3" is read as at least 3 theses in the earlier window; the threshold `min_decline` is an absolute difference of shares and must be declared. A thesis outside the windows, or a window with no thesis, is refused. 2026 may be a partial year; shares mitigate this, but its window is smaller.
      11. Stability matching: topics differ per seed, so gaps are matched by the doc codes of the theses involved. Two gaps can match only within one rule and, for (c), the same source and target; their overlap is the Jaccard index of their topic's members, or, for a (b) pair, the overlap of the pair's weaker topic in whichever order of the two topics is better; a gap is present in a run when some gap of that run overlaps it by at least `min_overlap` (required, no default; its value must be declared). The matching is not one to one.
      12. Stability reference: the filter assesses the gaps of one reference run, the run whose map is shown (D30's medoid run), and keeps those present in at least `gaps.stability_min_runs` (4) of the 5 runs, the reference included. A gap absent from the reference run is not reported even when present in the other 4, since its topics are not on the shown map.
      13. The loader imports `_UniqueKeyLoader`, a private name of `shared/config.py`, to reuse the project's duplicate-key refusal instead of writing a second one; exposing it under a public name would be a one-line change in the configuration module.
      14. No configuration change: the values that the plan does not declare were not added to `gaps:`, since choosing them is a user decision recorded as a configuration row; the existing pins (`test_experiment_declaration.py`, `test_career_affinity.py`) are untouched.

  - Implementation notes for the user to confirm:
    1. The four sensitivity values (`LITERAL_SMALL_PERCENT` 5, `LITERAL_DISTANCE_QUANTILE` 0.75, `RARE_MAX_SHARE` 0.01, `MIN_OVERLAP` 0.75) are constants of `gaps/sensitivity.py`, since D32 states them in its text and `gaps:` holds only the declared values; a test pins them and the manifest records them under `sensitivity`.
    2. The sensitivity table's columns: `variant`, `setting`, `rule`, `run_id`, `seed`, `reference`, `gaps`, `theses`, `noise_theses`, `stable` and `jaccard_to_reference`; one row per variant, rule it touches and run. The variants are `primary` (the declared values, the baseline of the comparison), `literal_a`, `literal_b`, `rare_max_share` (rule (c) only) and `min_overlap` (the four rules, the same gaps as `primary`); each changes one value.
    3. Literal (a): "fewer than 5 %" is strict and exact in integers (744 theses: at most 37; 740: at most 36), with no NPMI condition. It goes through `small_coherent_topics` with the floor at -1, so a topic with an undefined NPMI is not counted; C4's runs have none (92 topics, 92 defined, M). Its gaps go through the declared stability filter.
    4. Literal (b) flags theses, not gaps: per run, the mean cosine distance of each thesis, noise included, to the run's topic centroids, strictly above the run's P75 (`numpy.quantile`'s linear interpolation). Its stability is the Jaccard index of each run's flagged theses with the reference run's; it has no stable count, and the flagged theses are not written, only counted.
    5. Refusals beyond the spec: the configuration's experiment declaration must be T19's `declaration_sha256` (so the configured seeds are T19's); the input manifests read by T19's loader must equal T19's `inputs`; `selection.json` and T19's manifest must name the same winner; the medoid run must be one of its seed runs; each topic's size in `topics.parquet` must equal its count in `labels.parquet`; the labels must cover the document vectors' theses exactly, once each; every thesis needs an issue year (rule (d)).
    6. The winner's seed runs are named by T19's `run_id` over the configured seeds, since `selection.json` lists none; `runs.parquet` is not read.
    7. The run lock is taken on `results/gaps/<snapshot>/` before any input is read, so a refused real run leaves that directory with only its gitignored `.lock`. T19's runner takes no lock.
    8. The privacy guard reads the tables as the JSON of their rows, and `cuts.json` and `summary.md` as written; the manifest is checked before any write (with its digests empty) and again just before it is written. The dry run checks the four other outputs and exits 1 on a hit. The error names each file and its count of hits, never the strings.
    9. `gap_id`: `<run_id>__a_t<topic>`, `<run_id>__b_t<first>-t<second>`, `<run_id>__c_t<topic>_<source>-<target>` and `<run_id>__d_t<topic>`, unique within a run by the rules' definitions. In `members.parquet`, `part` is `topic`, or `first_topic`, `second_topic` and `bridge` for a rule (b) pair.
    10. A gap's `programs`, `years` and `theses` count the theses of its topics (both topics of a rule (b) pair); its bridge theses are counted in `bridge_theses` only.
    11. The manifest's `versions` are T19's `library_versions()` as they are (Python and nine libraries, numpy and pyarrow among them); pydantic and PyYAML are not listed, and `uv.lock` at the recorded commit pins them. Its `inputs` are by file name: T19's three input manifests, `metadata.jsonl` (the SHA-256 that the snapshot manifest records and `read_snapshot` checks), `default.yaml` and `career_affinity.yaml`; the fichas table is pinned through `fichas_manifest.json`.
    12. `summary.md` lists only the reference run's stable gaps, each with its id, support, reason and the top-10 T19 terms of its topics, and counts rule (c)'s affinity-2 group.
    13. Unlike T19's, this `--dry-run` reads every input and computes everything (about 10 s, M), so its numbers are those the real run will write.

- [x] **T26** Recommender: antecedents (top-k with MMR) and alternative lines (neighbor topics plus nearby gaps).
  - Route D. Risk M.
  - Route declaration: 2026-10-19 overnight: delegated direct, one writer in an isolated worktree; trigger: parallel units. The writer's run was cut by an API rate limit after the antecedents were GREEN and resumed on 2026-10-20 in the same worktree, with no file lost.
  - Depends on T22 (`redundancy/search.py`), T23 (`topic_map/assignment.py`) and T24 (`gaps/rules.py`, `gaps/stability.py`), which were copied into the worktree as untracked files and not modified. T26 merges after those three units.
  - Design (plan §3.6.7: suggest the most relevant antecedents by semantic similarity and propose related but distinctive thematic lines; `recommender`: top-k 5, MMR λ 0.7, 3 neighbor topics, 3 nearby gaps):
    - Antecedents (`recommendation/antecedents.py`): Maximal Marginal Relevance (Carbonell and Goldstein, 1998), `argmax_d [λ·sim(q,d) − (1−λ)·max_{s∈S} sim(d,s)]`, picked one at a time. `sim` is T22's cosine in the original space (`cosine_top_k` for the proposal, `cosine_scores` between theses). The empty set's redundancy term is 0, so the first pick is always the thesis most similar to the proposal. The term is the true maximum, never floored at 0, since cosines can be negative. Exact ties go to the higher similarity to the proposal, then to the lower row. λ = 1 equals T22's plain top-k exactly, and λ = 0 takes the least redundant thesis at each step. Dense SBERT vectors and scipy sparse TF-IDF matrices are both supported through T22's cosine. Each result has its rank, its row, its similarity to the proposal, its redundancy (`None` for the first pick) and its MMR score at the step that picked it.
    - Alternative lines (`recommendation/alternatives.py`): the assigned topic is T23's `assign_proposal` on `TopicCentroids.from_topic_map`. The neighbor topics are the `neighbor_topics` topics with the highest centroid cosine to a reference, which is either the proposal or the assigned topic's centroid. The assigned topic is left out, and an exact tie goes to the lower label. Noise has no centroid on a `TopicMap`, so it is never a candidate. The nearby gaps are T24's stable gaps, ranked by the cosine distance `1 − cos` between the proposal and the gap's anchor. A one-topic gap is anchored at its topic's centroid. A rule (b) pair is anchored at the midpoint of its two unit centroids or at the nearer of the two. A gap must be within `max_distance` when one is given, inclusive, and an exact tie keeps the given order. Each neighbor carries its similarity, size, c-TF-IDF terms and a `reason`. Each gap carries its rule, topics, anchor topic, similarity, distance, its `GapSupport` and a `reason` built on T24's own reason. No result holds a thesis text.
  - Public API:
    - `rank_antecedents(documents, proposal, *, k, mmr_lambda, pool_size, exclude) -> AntecedentRanking`, returning `Antecedent(rank, index, similarity, redundancy, mmr_score)`. `AntecedentRanking` holds `antecedents`, `k`, `mmr_lambda`, `pool_size`, `candidates` and `exclude`. Invalid arguments raise `RecommendationError(ValueError)`.
    - `alternative_lines(proposal, topic_map, gaps, *, neighbor_topics, nearby_gaps, neighbor_reference, pair_anchor, max_distance) -> AlternativeLines`.
      - `AlternativeLines` holds `assignment`, `assigned_size`, `assigned_terms`, `neighbors`, `gaps`, `neighbor_reference`, `pair_anchor` and `max_distance`.
      - `NeighborTopic(rank, topic, similarity, size, terms, assigned_topic, reference)` has a `.reason`.
      - `NearbyGap(rank, rule, topics, anchor_topic, similarity, distance, support)` has a `.reason`.
      - The literals are `NeighborReference` (`"proposal"`, `"assigned_topic"`) and `PairAnchor` (`"midpoint"`, `"nearer_topic"`).
    - The configured values come from `config.recommender` (`top_k`, `mmr_lambda`, `neighbor_topics`, `nearby_gaps`); a test pins 5, 0.7, 3 and 3. No new settings class was added.
  - Implementation notes for the user to confirm (each is an explicit argument with no default unless stated):
    1. Candidate pool, `pool_size`. `None` makes the whole corpus the candidates; an integer N makes the N theses most similar to the proposal the candidates. Recommended: `None`. The cost is trivial at 744 theses: k = 5 passes over at most 744 rows. A top-N pool (N about 50) would stop a low λ from reaching far-off theses.
    2. Proposal row, `exclude`. The caller names the proposal's own row when the proposal is a thesis of the corpus. An identical copy in another row is still a candidate, as in T22's search.
    3. Results are corpus rows, as in T22's `Neighbor`, not doc codes. The app maps the rows to fichas.
    4. Sparse support. Antecedents accept dense or sparse vectors. Alternative lines take a dense proposal vector only, since T23's `assign_proposal` and the `TopicMap` centroids are dense.
    5. Neighbor reference, `neighbor_reference`. With `"proposal"`, neighbor 1 is T23's runner-up, and relatedness is measured to the proposal itself. With `"assigned_topic"`, nearness is rule (b)'s notion between topics, and every proposal of a topic gets the same neighbors. Recommended: `"proposal"`.
    6. Gap anchor for a rule (b) pair, `pair_anchor`. `"midpoint"` uses the normalized sum of the two unit centroids, the direction rule (b) measures its bridges against. `"nearer_topic"` uses the nearer centroid; when the two are exactly as near, it uses the first, lower topic. Recommended: `"midpoint"`, since a rule (b) gap is the region between the two topics. A pair with opposite centroids has no midpoint and raises.
    7. "Nearby" means the `nearby_gaps` stable gaps of smallest cosine distance, filtered by `max_distance` (inclusive; `None` means no limit). Recommended: `None`, because only stable gaps (D16) are candidates and only 3 are shown.
    8. Only stable `GapSupport` objects are accepted (D16). An unstable gap, or a gap naming a topic the map lacks, raises `RecommendationError` instead of being dropped silently. The caller passes `stable_gaps(...)` of the reference (medoid) run whose map is shown.
    9. Gaps that share a topic count separately: rule (c) gives one gap per program pair, and rules (a) and (d) can mark the same topic. The 3 nearby gaps can therefore name one topic. An alternative is grouping gaps by anchor. Rule (c) gaps are not filtered by the proposal's program; filtering for a proposal from the target program would be a refinement.
    10. `RecommendationError` is defined in `antecedents.py`, because `recommendation/__init__.py` was outside the edit surface. It can move to `__init__.py` at merge.
  - RED/GREEN:
    - RED, against stubs: `uv run --offline pytest tests/unit/recommendation -q`: 56 failed in 6.97 s, all `NotImplementedError` (M).
    - GREEN, antecedents, first run: 1 failed and 27 passed. This was a real defect: the redundancy running max started at 0 and floored negative cosines. It was caught by the λ = 0 property test and fixed by starting the max from the first pick's cosines. Then 28 passed (M).
    - GREEN, alternatives, first run: 1 failed and 55 passed. This was a fixture defect: maps built from trigonometric angles give no exact float tie (cos 90° came out as 6e-17). The tie test now uses axis-aligned vectors. Then 56 passed (M).
    - Final: 56 passed in 4.72–6.09 s over the runs (M). Of these, 28 are in `test_mmr_antecedents.py` and 28 in `test_alternative_lines.py` (M).
  - Mutants: 20 throwaway mutants, run with `-p no:cacheprovider` and `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`. All 20 were killed, and each file was restored byte-identical (SHA-256 checked after every mutant) (M). Pytest time per mutant run was 4.01–6.59 s, 98.66 s in all (D, sum of the run outputs):
    - M01: λ and 1−λ swapped.
    - M02: max replaced by mean in the redundancy term.
    - M03: proposal not excluded.
    - M04 and M05: k off by one, +1 and −1.
    - M06: running max floored at 0.
    - M07: MMR tie goes to the last candidate.
    - M08: pool size ignored.
    - M09: the first pick records redundancy 0.
    - M10: λ above 1 accepted.
    - M11: neighbors include the assigned topic.
    - M12: noise as a neighbor. Noise is structurally absent, so the mutant injects the noise class as a candidate.
    - M13: neighbor tie goes to the higher label.
    - M14: neighbor reference swapped.
    - M15: gap distance sign flipped.
    - M16: `nearer_topic` takes the farther topic.
    - M17: `max_distance` made exclusive.
    - M18: unstable gaps accepted.
    - M19: gap ties in reverse order.
    - M20: midpoint replaced by the first topic.

Metrics: ARI (the committed `seed_stability`), NMI (scikit-learn, arithmetic mean) and the counterpart agreement, the only new metric: a topic's counterpart is learned from its own earlier members only, so no assigned thesis picks its own counterpart; ties go to the lower label. The earlier theses on both maps get ARI and NMI only (their agreement would be in-sample), as context for how much the map itself moves.

Alternative: exclude them from the later period.
    7. The sample: 40 theses, 10 from the atypical pool (all when fewer) and 30 typical, each pool spread over the 5 programs with a floor of 2 by largest remainder, uniform within each stratum, presented in a seeded order so that a rater cannot tell a pool by its position; seed 20261027. Reasons: 6 to 8 experts (U6) rating about 40 items fits one session at about a minute each (E); a "fits" share near 0.8 over 40 items has a 95 % half-width of about 0.12 (D, 1.96 × √(0.16 / 40)); the atypical pool tests O06's flag; the floor gives every program at least 2 items. `sample.parquet` holds the position, doc code, title, program, year, future flag, pool, topic with its size and top terms, similarity, threshold, atypical flag, runner-up and margin, full-map label and `counterpart_agrees`; T31 decides what the experts see.

    9. Records: tracked `runs`, `consistency`, `topics` (top terms, vocabulary lemmas as T19's `topics.parquet` holds them, with each topic's counterparts and later counts), `composition`, `summary.md` and `manifest.json`; gitignored `assignments.parquet` (every thesis on every seed's maps) and `sample.parquet`. The privacy guard adds the doc codes, as T20a's does.

  - Implementation notes for the user to confirm (T28a):
    1. The 2-D layout is T19's `map.parquet` of the representative run (seed 42, UMAP through T16's `project`); no build command was added.
    2. URLs: the map is the default page, served at `http://localhost:<port>/`; the gaps page is `/vacios`. `/mapa` is not a route and shows Streamlit's own English "Page not found" dialog before the map, a text the app cannot translate; the README gives the two URLs.
    3. The gaps' program filter matches a program when the gap's topics hold theses of it or, under rule (c), when it is the source or the target, so a student of the target program finds the topics missing there (help text on the filter).
    4. The map's topic filter shows exactly the chosen topics; the switch for the theses without a topic applies only with no topic chosen and is disabled otherwise; the theses table lists the noise last.
    5. Colors: an eight-hue categorical palette by topic id (checked with the dataviz validator: light and dark pass every adjacent-pair check; light has three hues under 3:1 contrast, relieved by the labels and tables), three marker shapes for topics 8–15 and 16–23, the topic number at each topic's median; with 17 topics no palette separates every pair by hue alone.
    6. Caching is in the server's memory only, keyed by project root and snapshot, with no automatic invalidation when an artifact is rebuilt while the server runs; the sidebar's button "Volver a cargar los resultados guardados" clears it.
    7. Titles are shown by design, with their repository links; the app does not run a name guard on them (D34 (1) measured no person-name form in the 744 titles, and the real run's scan found none in everything rendered).
    8. Cross-record checks beyond the manifests' SHA-256: the gap run must name the T19 manifest read; the fichas manifest must be the one T19 ran on (otherwise "stale", and the message suggests rerunning T19); the map must be the selection's map run; the topic sizes must equal the map's counts; every thesis must have a ficha.

### P7 — Validation (M6–M7)
- [ ] **T30** V de Aiken computation and finalization of the questionnaire with the judges (U6).
  - Route I. Risk P.
  - V de Aiken computation built and tested on synthetic data (2026-10-19 overnight; moved into the main tree 2026-10-20): `evaluation/aiken.py`, 50 tests. Open (U6): the judges, their consent, the rating scale and criteria, and finalizing the questionnaire with the judges; the cut-off (N7) is decided (D31). T30 stays unchecked. Route declaration, sources, test-first evidence, mutants and checks are under T21 (one unit for T21, T30 and T32).
  - What is built, `src/thematic_redundancy/evaluation/aiken.py` (250 lines, M):
    - `aiken_v(ratings, *, low, high) -> float | None`: `V = S / (n (c - 1))`, where `S = Σ (r - low)` over the ratings given, `n` counts them and `c = high - low + 1`. `None` marks a missing rating; with no rating at all the result is `None`.
    - `aiken_score_interval(v, *, judges, categories, confidence) -> (lower, upper)`: the score interval `(2NV + z² ∓ z √(4NV(1 − V) + z²)) / (2(N + z²))`, with `N = n (c − 1)` and `z = Φ⁻¹((1 + confidence) / 2)`. The bounds are clipped so that the interval holds V and lies in [0, 1] under rounding; the first GREEN run gave an upper bound of 0.9999999999999999 at V = 1 (M), and the clipping fixed it.
    - `aiken_validity(ratings, dimensions, *, low, high, confidence, criterion, cutoff) -> AikenValidity`: per item (`AikenItem`), `judges` given, `missing`, `distance_sum` (S), `v`, `ci_lower`, `ci_upper` and `accepted`, where `accepted` is criterion value ≥ cutoff and the criterion is `"v"` or `"ci_lower"`; per dimension (`AikenDimension`), the mean and the minimum V, and the accepted, rejected and undefined items. An item with no rating is `"no_ratings"`, and its dimension's mean is `"undefined_items"`. Items are listed in the order of `dimensions`.
    - The criterion, the cut-off and the confidence level are keyword-only, with no default.
  - Implementation notes for the user to confirm:
    - N6. Judges' scale and criteria. The plan fixes neither the judges' rating scale nor its points. §3.8.2 names clarity, relevance and sufficiency. Run `aiken_validity` once per criterion. Sufficiency, often judged per dimension, can be passed as one "item" per dimension.
    - N7. Acceptance rule. The criterion (`"v"` or `"ci_lower"`), the cut-off and the confidence level have no default. No literature cut-off was verified offline. Values often quoted (for example 0.70 for V, or a lower bound of 0.50 or 0.70 at 95%) are from memory and must be checked in a primary source before they are cited or declared. Small panels matter: with V = 1 the lower bound at 95% is 0.6097 for 2 judges on a 1–4 scale and 0.7009 for 3 judges (M, from the tests), so a `"ci_lower"` rule at 0.70 needs at least 3 unanimous judges on that scale. **Decided 2026-10-20 (D31):** `criterion="ci_lower"`, `cutoff=0.50`, `confidence=0.95`; V and both bounds are reported per item.
    - N8. Missing ratings. They are left out, so `n` is the number of ratings given for that item. The alternative is to require every judge's rating; that check belongs to the caller.
    - N9. Dimension mean. It is the unweighted mean of the items' V, and it is undefined when any item has no rating.
    - N10. Citations to verify; no primary text was available offline:
      - Penfield and Giacobbi (2004), "Applying a score confidence interval to Aiken's item content-relevance index", *Measurement in Physical Education and Exercise Science* 8(4), 213–225: the formula and the reference are from memory, **to verify**. Verified here: the formula is algebraically Wilson's score interval with `N = n(c − 1)` trials, and it equals scipy 1.18.1's `proportion_ci(method="wilson")` to 1e-12 for every S over 12 settings, (judges, c) ∈ {(3,4), (5,5), (8,4), (7,2)} × confidence ∈ {0.90, 0.95, 0.99} (M). scipy cites Wilson, E. B. (1927), "Probable inference, the law of succession, and statistical inference", *J. Amer. Stat. Assoc.* 22, 209–212.
      - Aiken (1985), "Three coefficients for analyzing the reliability and validity of ratings", *Educational and Psychological Measurement* 45(1), 131–142: the formula V = S / (n(c − 1)) is standard, but the bibliographic details are from memory, **to verify**.
      - Checked 2026-10-20 (D31): Merino-Soto and Livia-Segovia (2009), *Anales de Psicología* 25(1), 169–171, full text read, compute this score interval following Penfield and Giacobbi (2004), whose bibliographic data above match their reference list. `aiken_score_interval` reproduces their second example exactly (14 judges, 1–5 scale, V = 0.8, 90 %: [0.70, 0.87]; M: 0.6992, 0.8732). Their first example, printed as 95 % [0.62, 0.90], matches the 90 % interval (M: 0.6216, 0.9069) and not the 95 % one (M: 0.5840, 0.9193), likely a misprint. Aiken (1985): the data above match several sources, with DOI 10.1177/0013164485451012; the full text was not read. Escurra (1988), *Revista de Psicología* (PUCP) 6(1–2), 103–111, often cited in Peru for a 0.80 cut-off: that figure was not verified, and Merino-Soto and Livia-Segovia list it as 1989.

- [ ] **T32** Questionnaire analysis (medians, frequencies, Cronbach α) and the check against success criteria a–d.
  - Route D. Risk M.
  - Analysis functions built and tested on synthetic data (2026-10-19 overnight; moved into the main tree 2026-10-20): `evaluation/likert.py`, 40 tests. Open: the check against success criteria a–d, which needs the real answers and T19's and T22's results for (a), (b) and (d); the reading of (c) is decided (N12, D31). T32 stays unchecked. Route declaration, sources, test-first evidence, mutants and checks are under T21 (one unit for T21, T30 and T32).
  - What is built, `src/thematic_redundancy/evaluation/likert.py` (357 lines, M):
    - `likert_analysis(responses, dimensions, *, low, high, reverse_coded, quartile_method) -> LikertAnalysis`: per item (`ItemSummary`), the frequency of every scale point (zeros included), the missing answers counted apart, and the median, quartiles and IQR of the answers given (`Quartiles`); per dimension (`DimensionSummary`), the same over all its answers pooled, the median and quartiles of each complete respondent's mean score, Cronbach's α (`CronbachAlpha`) and the α-if-item-deleted table (`AlphaIfItemDeleted`). Reverse-coded items are recoded as `low + high − a` before every statistic.
    - `cronbach_alpha(scores) -> CronbachAlpha`: the input is a respondents × items matrix, with `NaN` for a missing answer; the formula is `k/(k−1) · (1 − Σ item variances / total variance)`, with the sample variance (ddof 1) in both places; missing answers are handled by listwise deletion, and the used and excluded respondents are reported. Undefined cases: `"fewer_than_two_items"`, `"fewer_than_two_respondents"` and `"zero_total_variance"`. A negative α is reported as a value.
    - `answer_matrix` and `item_dimensions` are the public validators of an answer table and its dimensions; `aiken.py` reuses them (N15, under T21).
  - Implementation notes for the user to confirm:
    - N11. Reverse coding. It applies to every statistic, the frequencies included. The draft in Anexo A has only affirmative items ("cuatro ítems afirmativos", §3.8.2), so the expected set is empty. It stays an explicit argument.
    - N12. Which median decides criterion (c). The criterion says each dimension "obtiene una mediana igual o superior a cuatro", which can be read three ways, and all three are reported: the median of all the dimension's answers pooled (`DimensionSummary.answers.median`); the median of each complete respondent's mean over the dimension's items (`respondent_scores.median`); the item medians (`ItemSummary.answers.median`). Choose the reading ex ante, before the expert answers arrive. **Decided 2026-10-20 (D31):** the median over the experts of each complete expert's mean (`respondent_scores.median`) decides; the pooled median and the item medians are reported as complements.
    - N13. Quartile method. It is an explicit argument, one of numpy's nine Hyndman–Fan methods (Hyndman and Fan, 1996, *The American Statistician* 50(4), 361–365, as cited by numpy). Proposal: `"linear"` (type 7, numpy's default). The median is always numpy's median.
    - N14. Cronbach's α. It uses listwise deletion; pingouin's default is pairwise, and its docstring says listwise matches R psych's `use="complete.obs"`. The α-if-item-deleted table uses the same complete respondents. No confidence interval is computed; pingouin offers Feldt's interval if one is wanted. With 6–8 experts α is unstable, as §3.8.2 already warns. Cronbach (1951) is cited from memory: **to verify** before citing.

## Feature acceptance criteria (plan §3.6.8, adapted)

1. The winning semantic configuration beats the TF-IDF baseline on NPMI coherence and on labeled-set F1. Silhouette is reported within each space and is not compared across spaces. Under D30 (UD1): on NPMI by more than the larger seed SD, both at the baseline's own K and at the matched K; on F1 with a paired 95% bootstrap interval of the difference that excludes 0.
2. Direct-redundancy F1 is ≥ 0.70 at the selected τ, cross-validated and reported with a bootstrap CI.
3. Each of the 4 questionnaire dimensions has a median ≥ 4.
4. The winning configuration is stable across the 5 seeds (low SD, with ARI reported). Under D30 (UD2): a mean pairwise ARI ≥ 0.65 (`own_label`), and a coefficient of variation over the seeds ≤ 0.10 for silhouette and for NPMI.
5. Engineering: lint, format and tests all pass. The app runs from a clean clone plus the snapshot, and the Docker image runs with mounted data.

If a criterion is not met, that is reported as a finding. It is never hidden.

## Checks

- `uv run ruff check .`
- `uv run ruff format --check .`
- `uv run pytest -q`
- Test-first: deterministic modules follow RED → GREEN → REFACTOR with `uv run pytest`. Exceptions: EDA notebooks, experiment runs, manual labeling, and the Docker run check.
- Processing costs: every long or measured operation adds a row to `docs/processing_costs.md` in the same work unit.

## Risks

| ID | Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Objectives extraction falls below the target | Fall back to title + abstract and document it (O05) |
| R2 | Fresh library majors break BERTopic | Smoke test in T02; pin the previous major |
| R3 | Low annotator agreement | A guide with examples, a 10-pair pilot, and re-labeling of disputed pairs |
| R4 | F1 < 0.70, or another criterion unmet | Report it honestly as a result |
| R5 | Industrial dominates the topics (46% of the corpus) | Report per program and normalize gap rules per program |
| R6 | Annotators or experts are unavailable | Request them now (U3, U6) |
| R7 | Cross-lingual matching against OpenAlex is weak | Use nearest-topic assignment and manually check a sample |
| R8 | The snapshot is lost | Accepted by the user, with no backup (D23). The manifests' uuids and sha256 allow a documented re-harvest |

## Delivery strategy

- **Forecast:** about 8,000–9,000 authored changed lines (source plus tests), which exceeds the ~400-line slice budget.
- **Strategy:** direct work-unit commits on `main`, with no PR chain (O08, user decision). Slices are commits, each one coherent on its own.
- **Commits:** Conventional Commits only, with no trailers. The user writes the Summary and Description in Spanish, and the type keywords stay in English (user decision, 2026-10-02). Pushing is the user's decision.
- **Slice boundaries (commits per task):** recorded in each task's evidence.

5. **Open, non-blocking.** Implementation notes that wait for the user's confirmation: T19 (three of the first slice, eight of the second), T22 (ten; its notes 2 to 5 and 7 are now declared in `redundancy` and wait for SR-T22-1), T22's run command (fourteen), T27 (thirteen), T20b (twelve), T26 (ten), T24a (thirteen), T20a (ten), T31 (twenty-one), T28a (fifteen), T28b (fourteen) and T12c items 6 and 10 (the run lock around the write only; only the candidates file left out of the stopword script's uncommitted-changes check). T34 lists the changes to the thesis document, D31's and D32's among them. The 2026-10-27 speed run's own decisions for the user (SR-T22-1 to SR-T22-3, SR-T27-1, SR-T27-2 and SR-T20b-1, among others) wait in its dossier, kept outside the repository.

Pending user inputs: U1; U2's UD1 part (whether the advisor was told); U3 (month 3: the number of annotators, 3 or 4, D34 (8)); U5; U6; U8 for any new download (the OpenAlex topics for T25 and, optionally, the 7 expired-embargo PDFs); the date of the calibration meeting (D34 (7)). The T10c redactor policy and the pilot size were decided on 2026-10-21 (D34 (1) and (7)).
